\text{where }x:0\vdash E:T\end{array}

\times

{\texttt{split}\,\,(V_{1},V_{2})\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2}).E\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}E[V_{1}/x_{1},V_{2}/x_{2}]}

\begin{array}[]{l}E\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{split}\,\,x\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2}).E[(x_{1},x_{2})/x]\\
\text{where }{x:A_{1}\times A_{2}\vdash E:T}\end{array}

1

\texttt{split}\,\,()\,\texttt{to}\,\,().E\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}E

\begin{array}[]{l}{x:1\vdash E\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{split}\,\,x\,\texttt{to}\,\,().E[()/x]:T}\\
\text{where }{x:1\vdash E:T}\end{array}

U

{\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}M}

{x:U\underline{B}\vdash x\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{force}\,\,x:U\underline{B}}

F

{\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,V;M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}M[V/x]}

{\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\bullet;M[\texttt{ret}\,\,x/\bullet]:\underline{B}}

\to

{(\lambda x:A.M)\,V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}M[V/x]}

{\bullet:A\to\underline{B}\vdash\bullet\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\lambda x:A.\bullet\,x:A\to\underline{B}}

\mathbin{\&}

\begin{array}[]{l}{\pi\{\pi\mapsto{M}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{M^{\prime}}\}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}M}\\
{\pi^{\prime}\{\pi\mapsto{M}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{M^{\prime}}\}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}M^{\prime}}\end{array}

{\bullet:\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}\vdash\bullet\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\pi\mapsto{\pi\bullet}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\pi^{\prime}\bullet}\}:\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}}

\top

-

{\bullet:\top\vdash\bullet\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\}:\top}

\framebox{Error Properties}\hskip 18.49988pt\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}\mid\cdot\vdash M^{\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}}{\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime}\mid\cdot\vdash\mho\sqsubseteq M^{\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{lab=ErrBot}\hskip 18.49988pt\frac{\Gamma\mid x:\underline{B}\vdash S:\underline{B}^{\prime}}{\Gamma\mid\cdot\vdash S[\mho_{\underline{B}}]\sqsubseteq\mho_{\underline{B^{\prime}}}:\underline{B}^{\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{lab=StkStrict}

Figure 5.
GTT Term Dynamism Axioms

3.
Theorems in Gradual Type Theory

In this section, we show that the axiomatics of gradual type theory
determine most properties of casts, which shows that these behaviors of
casts are forced in any implementation of gradual typing satisfying
graduality and
\beta,\eta
.

3.1.
Properties inherited from CBPV

Because the GTT term equidynamism relation
\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}
includes the
congruence and
\beta\eta
axioms of the CBPV equational theory, types
inherit the universal properties they have there
(
Levy 2003
)
. We recall
some relevant definitions and facts.

Definition 3.1 (Isomorphism).

(1)

We write
A\cong_{v}A^{\prime}
for a
value isomorphism between

A
and
A^{\prime}
, which consists of two complex values
x:A\vdash V^{\prime}:A^{\prime}
and
x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\vdash V:A
such that
x:A\vdash V[V^{\prime}/x^{\prime}]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}x:A
and
x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\vdash V^{\prime}[V/x]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}
.

(2)

We write
\underline{B}\cong_{c}\underline{B}^{\prime}
for a
computation
isomorphism between
\underline{B}
and
\underline{B}^{\prime}
, which consists of two
complex stacks
\bullet:\underline{B}\vdash S^{\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}
and
\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash S:\underline{B}
such that
\bullet:\underline{B}\vdash S[S^{\prime}/x^{\prime}]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\bullet:\underline{B}
and
\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash S^{\prime}[S/\bullet]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}
.

Note that a value isomorphism is a strong condition, and an isomorphism
in call-by-value between types
A
and
A^{\prime}
corresponds to a computation
isomorphism
\underline{F}A\cong\underline{F}A^{\prime}
, and dually
(
Levy 2017
)
.

Lemma 3.2 (Initial objects).

(1)

For all (value or computation) types
T
, there exists a unique
expression
x:0\vdash E:T
.

(2)

For all
\underline{B}
, there exists a unique stack
\bullet:\underline{F}0\vdash S:\underline{B}
.

(3)

0 is strictly initial: Suppose there is a type
A
with a complex
value
x:A\vdash V:0
. Then
V
is an isomorphism
A\cong_{v}0
.

(4)

\underline{F}0
is not provably
strictly
initial among computation types.

Proof.

(1)

Take
E
to be
x:0\vdash\texttt{abort}\,\,{x}:T
. Given any
E^{\prime}
,
we have
E\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}E^{\prime}
by the
\eta
principle for
0
.

(2)

Take
S
to be
\bullet:\underline{F}0\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{abort}\,\,{x}}:\underline{B}
. Given another
S^{\prime}
,
by the
\eta
principle for
F
types,
S^{\prime}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\bullet;{S^{\prime}[\texttt{ret}\,\,x]}
. By congruence, to show
S\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}S^{\prime}
, it suffices to show
x:0\vdash\texttt{abort}\,\,{x}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}S[\texttt{ret}\,\,{x}]:\underline{B}
, which is an instance of the previous part.

(3)

We have
y:0\vdash\texttt{abort}\,\,{y}:A
. The composite
y:0\vdash V[\texttt{abort}\,\,{y}/x]:0
is equidynamic with
y
by the
\eta

principle for
0
, which says that any two complex values with
domain
0
are equal.

The composite
x:A\vdash\texttt{abort}\,\,{V}:A
is equidynamic
with
x
, because

x:A,y:A,z:0\vdash x\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{abort}\,\,{z}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}y:A

where the first is by
\eta
with
x:A,y:A,z:0\vdash E[z]:=x:A
and the second with
x:0,y:0\vdash E[z]:=y:A
(this
depends on the fact that
0
is “distributive”, i.e.
\Gamma,x:0

has the universal property of
0
). Substituting
\texttt{abort}\,\,{V}
for
y

and
V
for
z
, we have
\texttt{abort}\,\,{V}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}x
.

(4)

\underline{F}0
is not
strictly
initial among computation types,
though. Proof sketch: a domain model along the lines of

(
New and Licata 2018
)
with only non-termination and type errors shows this,
because there
\underline{F}0
and
\top
are isomorphic (the same object is
both initial and terminal), so if
\underline{F}0
were strictly initial (any
type
\underline{B}
with a stack
\bullet:B\vdash S:\underline{F}0
is isomorphic
to
\underline{F}0
), then because every type
\underline{B}
has a stack to
\top

(terminal) and therefore
\underline{F}0
, every type would be isomorphic to

\top
/
\underline{F}0
—i.e. the stack category would be trivial. But there
are non-trivial computation types in this model.

∎

Lemma 3.3 (Terminal objects).

(1)

For any computation type
\underline{B}
, there exists a unique stack

\bullet:\underline{B}\vdash S:\top
.

(2)

(In any context
\Gamma
,) there exists a unique complex value

V:U\top
.

(3)

(In any context
\Gamma
,) there exists a unique complex value

V:1
.

(4)

U\top\cong_{v}1

(5)

\top
is not a strict terminal object.

Proof.

(1)

Take
S=\{\}
. The
\eta
rule for
\top
,
\bullet:\top\vdash\bullet\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\}:\top
, under the substitution of

\bullet:\underline{B}\vdash S:\top
, gives
S\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\}[S/\bullet]=\{\}
.

(2)

Take
V=\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\{\}}
. We have
x:U\top\vdash x\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\texttt{force}\,\,{x}}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\{\}}:U\top
by the

\eta
rules for
U
and
\top
.

(3)

Take
V=()
. By
\eta
for
1
with
x:1\vdash E[x]:=():1
, we have
x:1\vdash()\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{unroll}\,\,x\,\texttt{to}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,().:1
. By
\eta
fro
1
with
x:1\vdash E[x]:=x:1
, we have

x:1\vdash x\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{unroll}\,\,x\,\texttt{to}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,().
. Therefore
x:1\vdash x\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}():1
.

(4)

We have maps
x:U\top\vdash():1
and
x:1\vdash\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\{\}}:U\top
. The composite on
1
is the identity by
the previous part. The composite on
\top
is the identity by
part (2).

(5)

Proof sketch: As above, there is a domain model with

\top\cong\underline{F}0
, so if
\top
were a strict terminal object,
then
\underline{F}0
would be too. But
\underline{F}0
is also initial, so it
has a map to every type, and therefore every type would be
isomorphic to
\underline{F}0
and
\top
. But there are non-trivial
computation types in the model.

∎

3.2.
Derived Cast Rules

As noted above, monotonicity of type dynamism for
U
and
\underline{F}
means
that we have the following as instances of the general cast rules:

Lemma 3.4 (Shifted Casts).

The following are derivable:

\frac{\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash M:\underline{F}A^{\prime}A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}}{\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle M:\underline{F}A}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash V:U\underline{B}\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}}{\Gamma\vdash\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle V:U\underline{B}^{\prime}}

Proof.

They are instances of the general upcast and downcast rules, using the
fact that
U
and
\underline{F}
are congruences for type dynamism, so in the
first rule
\underline{F}A\sqsubseteq\underline{F}A^{\prime}
, and in the second,
U\underline{B}\sqsubseteq U\underline{B}^{\prime}
.∎

The cast universal properties in Figure
5

imply the following seemingly more general rules for reasoning about
casts:

Lemma 3.5 (Upcast and downcast left and right rules).

The following are derivable:

\frac{A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\Phi\vdash V\sqsubseteq V^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}}{\Phi\vdash V\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{V^{\prime}}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right=UpR}\par\frac{\Phi\vdash V\sqsubseteq V^{\prime\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}}{\Phi\vdash\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle V\sqsubseteq V^{\prime\prime}:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right=UpL}\par\frac{\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}\Phi\mid\Psi\vdash M^{\prime}\sqsubseteq M^{\prime\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}{\Phi\mid\Psi\vdash\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle M^{\prime}\sqsubseteq M^{\prime\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right=DnL}\par\frac{\Phi\mid\Psi\vdash M\sqsubseteq M^{\prime\prime}:B\sqsubseteq B^{\prime\prime}}{\Phi\mid\Psi\vdash M\sqsubseteq\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle M^{\prime\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right=DnR}

In sequent calculus terminology, an upcast is left-invertible, while a
downcast is right-invertible, in the sense that any time we have a
conclusion with a upcast on the left/downcast on the right, we can
without loss of generality apply these rules (this comes from upcasts
and downcasts forming a Galois connection). We write the
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}

and
\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}
premises on the non-invertible rules to
emphasize that the premise is not necessarily well-formed given that the
conclusion is.

Proof.

For upcast left, substitute
V^{\prime}
into the axiom
x\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{x}:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}
to get
V^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{V^{\prime}}
, and then use transitivity with the premise.

For upcast right, by transitivity of

x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\vdash\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\qquad x^{\prime}\sqsubseteq x^{\prime\prime}:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}\vdash x^{\prime}\sqsubseteq x^{\prime\prime}:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}

we have

x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}\vdash\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}\sqsubseteq x^{\prime\prime}:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}

Substituting the premise into this gives the conclusion.

For downcast left, substituting
M^{\prime}
into the axiom
\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\bullet:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
gives
\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{M}\sqsubseteq M
, and then transitivity with the premise gives
the result.

For downcast right, transitivity of

\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\quad\bullet^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\bullet^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle{\bullet^{\prime\prime}}

gives
\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\bullet\sqsubseteq\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle{\bullet^{\prime\prime}}
,
and then substitution of the premise into this gives the conclusion.∎

Though we did not include congruence rules for casts in
Figure
4
, it is derivable:

Lemma 3.6 (Cast congruence rules).

The following congruence rules for casts are derivable:

\frac{A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}}{x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\vdash\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{x^{\prime}}:A^{\prime\prime}}\frac{A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}}{x:A\vdash\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}}\par\frac{\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}{\bullet^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle{\bullet^{\prime\prime}}:\underline{B}}\frac{\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}{\bullet^{\prime\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle{\bullet^{\prime\prime}}\sqsubseteq\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle{\bullet^{\prime\prime}}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}}

Proof.

In all cases, uses the invertible and then non-invertible rule for the
cast. For the first rule, by upcast left, it suffices to show
x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\vdash{x}\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{x^{\prime}}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}

which is true by upcast right, using
x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}
in the premise.

For the second, by upcast left, it suffices to show

x:A\vdash{x}\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}
,
which is true by upcast right.

For the third, by downcast right, it suffices to show

\bullet^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq{\bullet^{\prime\prime}}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}
,
which is true by downcast left, using
\bullet^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime\prime}
in the premise.

For the fourth, by downcast right, it suffices show

\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle{\bullet^{\prime\prime}}\sqsubseteq{\bullet^{\prime\prime}}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}
,
which is true by downcast left.∎

3.3.
Type-generic Properties of Casts

The universal property axioms for upcasts and downcasts in
Figure
5
define them
uniquely
up to
equidynamism (
\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}
): anything with the same property
is behaviorally equivalent to a cast.

Theorem 3.7 (Specification for Casts is a Universal Property).

(1)

If
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
and
x:A\vdash V:A^{\prime}
is a complex value such that

{x:A\vdash x\sqsubseteq V:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}}

and

{x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\vdash V\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}}

then
x:A\vdash V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}:A^{\prime}
.

(2)

If
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
and
\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash S:\underline{B}
is a complex stack such that

{\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash S\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}}
and

{\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\bullet\sqsubseteq S:\underline{B}}

then
\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash S\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}

Proof.

For the first part, to show
\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}\sqsubseteq V
, by upcast
left, it suffices to show
x\sqsubseteq V:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
, which is one
assumption. To show
V\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}
, we substitute into
the second assumption with
x\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
,
which is true by upcast right.

For the second part, to show
S\sqsubseteq\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}
, by downcast right, it suffices to show
S\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
, which is one of the assumptions. To
show
\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq S
, we substitute into the
second assumption with
\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}
, which is true by downcast left.∎

Casts satisfy an identity and composition law:

Theorem 3.8 (Casts (de)composition).

For any
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}
and
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}
:

(1)

x:A\vdash\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle x\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}x:A

(2)

x:A\vdash\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle x\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle x:A^{\prime\prime}

(3)

\bullet:\underline{B}\vdash\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\bullet\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\bullet:\underline{B}

(4)

\bullet:\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\bullet\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\bullet)}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}

Proof.

We use Theorem
3.7
in all cases, and show that the
right-hand side has the universal property of the left.

(1)

Both parts expand to showing

x\sqsubseteq x:A\sqsubseteq A\vdash x\sqsubseteq x:A\sqsubseteq A
,
which is true by assumption.

(2)

First, we need to show
x\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle x)}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}
. By upcast right, it suffices to show
x\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
, which is also true by upcast right.

For
x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}\vdash\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle x)}\sqsubseteq x^{\prime\prime}
, by upcast left twice, it suffices to show
x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}
, which is true by assumption.

(3)

Both parts expand to showing
\bullet:\underline{B}\vdash\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet:\underline{B}
, which is true by assumption.

(4)

To show
\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\bullet\sqsubseteq\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\bullet)}
, by
downcast right (twice), it suffices to show
\bullet:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}\vdash{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}
, which is true by assumption. Next, we have to show
\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\bullet)}\sqsubseteq\bullet:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}
, and by downcast left, it suffices to show
\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\bullet:\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}
, which is
also true by downcast left.

∎

In particular, this composition property implies that the casts into and
out of the dynamic type are coherent, for example if
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}

then

\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}}
.

The following theorem says essentially that
x\sqsubseteq\langle{T}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{T^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{T^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{T}\rangle{x}}
(upcast then downcast might error
less but but otherwise does not change the behavior) and

\langle{T^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{T}\rangle{\langle{T}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{T^{\prime}}\rangle{x}}\sqsubseteq x
(downcast then upcast
might error more but otherwise does not change the behavior). However,
since a value type dynamism
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
induces a value upcast
x:A\vdash\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}:A^{\prime}
but a stack downcast
\bullet:\underline{F}A^{\prime}\vdash\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet}:\underline{F}A
(and dually for
computations), the statement of the theorem wraps one cast with
the constructors for
U
and
\underline{F}
types (functoriality of
\underline{F}/U
).

Theorem 3.9 (Casts are a Galois Connection).

(1)

\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{F}A^{\prime}\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}};{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})}}\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{F}A^{\prime}

(2)

\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash\bullet\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\bullet;{\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})})}}:\underline{F}A

(3)

x:U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash{\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{({\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{force}\,\,x}})})}}\sqsubseteq x:U\underline{B}^{\prime}

(4)

x:U\underline{B}\vdash x\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x})})})}:U\underline{B}

Proof.

(1)

By
\eta
for
F
types,
\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{F}A^{\prime}\vdash\bullet^{\prime}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet^{\prime};{\texttt{ret}\,\,{x^{\prime}}}:\underline{F}A^{\prime}
, so it
suffices to show

\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}};{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})}}\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet^{\prime};{\texttt{ret}\,\,{x^{\prime}}}

By congruence, it suffices to show
{\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}}\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{F}A\sqsubseteq\underline{F}A^{\prime}
, which
is true by downcast left, and

x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\vdash{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})}}\sqsubseteq{\texttt{ret}\,\,{x^{\prime}}}:A^{\prime}
,
which is true by congruence for
\mathsf{ret}
, upcast left, and the assumption.

(2)

By
\eta
for
F
types, it suffices to show

\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,\bullet\leftarrow x;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{x}}\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\bullet;{\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})})}}:\underline{F}A

so by congruence,

x:A\vdash\texttt{ret}\,\,{x}\sqsubseteq{\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})})}}

By downcast right, it suffices to show

x:A\vdash\texttt{ret}\,\,{x}\sqsubseteq(\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})}):\underline{F}A\sqsubseteq\underline{F}A^{\prime}

and by congruence

x:A\vdash x\sqsubseteq({(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})}):A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}

which is true by upcast right.

(3)

By
\eta
for
U
types, it suffices to show

x:U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash{\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{({\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{force}\,\,x}})})}}\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{x})}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}

By upcast left, it suffices to show

x:U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{({\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{force}\,\,x}})})}\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{x})}:U\underline{B}\sqsubseteq U\underline{B}^{\prime}

and by congruence

x:U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash{\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{force}\,\,x}}\sqsubseteq\texttt{force}\,\,{x}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}

which is true by downcast left.

(4)

By
\eta
for
U
types, it suffices to show

x:U\underline{B}\vdash\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,x)}\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x})})})}:U\underline{B}

and by congruence

x:U\underline{B}\vdash{(\texttt{force}\,\,x)}\sqsubseteq{(\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x})})})}:\underline{B}

By downcast right, it suffices to show

x:U\underline{B}\vdash{(\texttt{force}\,\,x)}\sqsubseteq{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x})})}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}

and by congruence

x:U\underline{B}\vdash{x}\sqsubseteq{(\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x})}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}

which is true by upcast right.

∎

The retract property says roughly that
x\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\langle{T^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{T}\rangle{\langle{T^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{T}\rangle{x}}
(upcast then downcast does not change
the behavior), strengthening the
\sqsubseteq
of
Theorem
3.9
. In
Figure
5
, we asserted the retract axiom for
casts with the dynamic type. This and the composition property implies
the retraction property for general casts:

Theorem 3.10 (Retract Property for General Casts).

(1)

\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\bullet;{\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})})}}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\bullet:\underline{F}A

(2)

x:U\underline{B}\vdash\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x})})})}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}x:U\underline{B}

Proof.

We need only to show the
\sqsubseteq
direction, because the converse is
Theorem
3.9
.

(1)

Substituting
\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})}
into
Theorem
3.9
’s

\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash\bullet\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\bullet;{\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})})}}:\underline{F}A

and
\beta
-reducing gives

x:A\vdash{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})}}\sqsubseteq{\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}{?}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}})})}}

Using this, after
\eta
-expanding
\bullet:\underline{F}A
on the right and using congruence for

\mathsf{bind}
, it suffices to derive as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})})}&\sqsubseteq&\text{ congruence }\\
\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}{?}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}})})}}&\sqsubseteq&\text{ composition }\\
\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}{?}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{{(\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})}})}&\sqsubseteq&\text{ retract axiom for $\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle$ }\\
\texttt{ret}\,\,{x}\\
\end{array}

(2)

After using
\eta
for
U
and congruence, it suffices to show

x:U\underline{B}\vdash\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x})})}\sqsubseteq\texttt{force}\,\,{x}:\underline{B}

Substituting
x:U\underline{B}\vdash{\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x}}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}

into Theorem
3.9
’s

x:U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash x\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{x})})})}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}

gives

x:U\underline{B}\vdash{\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x}}\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{{\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x}}})})})}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}

So we have

\begin{array}[]{lll}\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{{\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x}}})}&\sqsubseteq\\
\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{{\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x}}})})})})}}&\sqsubseteq&\beta\\
\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B^{\prime}}\rangle{(\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{{\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x}}})})})}&\sqsubseteq&\text{composition}\\
\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x})})}&\sqsubseteq&\text{retract axiom for $\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle$}\\
\texttt{ret}\,\,{x}&\sqsubseteq&\text{composition}\\
\end{array}

∎

3.4.
Unique Implementations of Casts

Definition 3.11.

Let a
type constructor

C
be a (value or computation) type that
well-formed according to the grammar in Figure
1
with
additional hypotheses
X\,\,\text{val type}
and
\underline{Y}\,\,\text{comp type}
standing for value
or computation types, respectively. We write
C[A/X]
and
C[\underline{B}/\underline{Y}]
for the substitution of a type for a variable.

For example,

\begin{array}[]{l}X_{1}\,\,\text{val type},X_{2}\,\,\text{val type}\vdash X_{1}+X_{2}\,\,\text{val type}\\
\underline{Y}\,\,\text{comp type}\vdash U\underline{Y}\,\,\text{val type}\\
X_{1}\,\,\text{val type},X_{2}\,\,\text{val type}\vdash\underline{F}(X_{1}+X_{2})\,\,\text{comp type}\end{array}

are type constructors.

It is admissible that all type constructors are monotone in type
dynamism, because we included a congruence rule for every type
constructor in Figure
2
:

Lemma 3.12 (Monotonicity of Type Constructors).

For any type constructor
X\,\,\text{val type}\vdash C
, if
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
then

C[A/X]\sqsubseteq C[A^{\prime}/x]
. For any type constructor
\underline{Y}\,\,\text{comp type}\vdash C
, if
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
then
C[\underline{B}/\underline{Y}]\sqsubseteq C[\underline{B}^{\prime}/\underline{Y}]
.

Proof.

Induction on
C
. In the case for a variable
X
or
\underline{Y}
,
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
or
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
by assumption. In all other cases, the
result follows from the inductive hypotheses and the congruence rule for
type dynamism for the type constructor
(Figure
2
). For example, in the case for
+
,

A_{1}[A/x]\sqsubseteq A_{1}[A^{\prime}/x]
and
A_{2}[A/x]\sqsubseteq A_{2}[A^{\prime}/x]
, so

A_{1}[A/x]+A_{2}[A/x]\sqsubseteq A_{1}[A^{\prime}/x]+A_{2}[A^{\prime}/x]
.
∎

The following lemma helps show that a complex value

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime}/X_{i},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}/\underline{Y}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i}/X_{i},\underline{B}_{i}/\underline{Y}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
is an
upcast from
C[A_{i}/X_{i},\underline{B}_{i}/\underline{Y}_{i}]
to
C[A_{i}^{\prime}/X_{i},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}/\underline{Y}_{i}]
.

Lemma 3.13 (Upcast Lemma).

Let
X_{1}\,\,\text{val type},\ldots X_{n}\,\,\text{val type},\underline{Y}_{1}\,\,\text{comp type},\ldots\underline{Y}_{n}\,\,\text{comp type}\vdash C\,\,\text{val type}
be a value type constructor. We abbreviate
the instantiation

C[A_{1}/X_{1},\ldots,A_{n}/X_{n},\underline{B}_{1}/\underline{Y}_{i},\ldots,\underline{B}_{m}/\underline{Y}_{m}]
by
C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]
.

Suppose
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{-}
is a complex
value (depending on
C
and each
A_{i},A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}
) such that

(1)

For all value types
A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}
and
A_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,A_{n}^{\prime}
with

A_{i}\sqsubseteq A_{i}^{\prime}
, and all computation types
\underline{B}_{1},\ldots,\underline{B}_{m}

and
\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\underline{B}_{n}^{\prime}
with
\underline{B}_{i}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}
,

x:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x}:C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]

(2)

For all value types
A_{i}\sqsubseteq A_{i}^{\prime}
and computation types
\underline{B}_{i}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}
,

\begin{array}[]{c}x:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{x}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{x}:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]\sqsubseteq C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]\\
x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]\sqsubseteq C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{x}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{x^{\prime}}:C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]\end{array}

(3)

For all value types
A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}
and all computation types

\underline{B}_{1},\ldots,\underline{B}_{m}
,

x:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}x:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]

Then
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
satisfies the
universal property of an upcast, so by Theorem
3.7

x:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle{x}:C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]

Moreover, the left-to-right direction uses only the left-to-right
direction of assumption (3), and the right-to-left uses only the
right-to-left direction of assumption (3).

Proof.

First, we show that
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle

satisfies the universal property of an upcast.

To show

x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\sqsubseteq{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x}\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}

assumption (2) part 2 gives

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x^{\prime}}:C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]

Then transitivity with the left-to-right direction of assumption (3)

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle{x}}\sqsubseteq{\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle{x}}

gives the result.

To show

{x}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x}:{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\sqsubseteq{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}

By assumption (2) part 1, we have

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]\sqsubseteq C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]

so transitivity with the right-to-left direction of assumption (3)
gives the result:

x\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x}

Then Theorem
3.7
implies that

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
is equivalent to

\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle
.
∎

Dually, we have

Lemma 3.14 (Downcast Lemma).

Let
X_{1}\,\,\text{val type},\ldots X_{n}\,\,\text{val type},\underline{Y}_{1}\,\,\text{comp type},\ldots\underline{Y}_{n}\,\,\text{comp type}\vdash C\,\,\text{comp type}
be a computation type constructor. We
abbreviate the instantiation

C[A_{1}/X_{1},\ldots,A_{n}/X_{n},\underline{B}_{1}/\underline{Y}_{i},\ldots,\underline{B}_{m}/\underline{Y}_{m}]
by
C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]
.

Suppose
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{-}
is a complex
stack (depending on
C
and each
A_{i},A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}
) such that

(1)

For all value types
A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}
and
A_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,A_{n}^{\prime}
with

A_{i}\sqsubseteq A_{i}^{\prime}
, and all computation types
\underline{B}_{1},\ldots,\underline{B}_{m}

and
\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\underline{B}_{n}^{\prime}
with
\underline{B}_{i}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}
,

\bullet:C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet}:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]

(2)

For all value types
A_{i}\sqsubseteq A_{i}^{\prime}
and computation types
\underline{B}_{i}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}
,

\begin{array}[]{c}\bullet:C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet}:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]\sqsubseteq C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]\\
\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]\sqsubseteq C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{x}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{x^{\prime}}:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]\end{array}

(3)

For all value types
A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}
and all computation types

\underline{B}_{1},\ldots,\underline{B}_{m}
,

\bullet:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\bullet:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]

Then
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
satisfies the
universal property of a downcast, so by Theorem
3.7

\bullet:C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle{\bullet}:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]

Moreover, the left-to-right direction uses only the left-to-right
direction of assumption (3), and the right-to-left uses only the
right-to-left direction of assumption (3).

Proof.

First, we show that
\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle

satisfies the universal property of a downcast, and then apply
Theorem
3.7
.
To show

\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]\sqsubseteq C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]\vdash\bullet\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]

assumption (2) part 2 gives

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}

Then transitivity with the right-to-left direction of assumption (3)

\bullet\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet}

gives the result.

To show

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\bullet:{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\sqsubseteq{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}

by assumption (2) part 1, we have

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet}:C[A_{i},\underline{B}_{i}]\sqsubseteq C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]

so transitivity with the left-to-right direction of assumption (3)

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C[A_{i}^{\prime},\underline{B}_{i}^{\prime}]}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\bullet

gives the result.
∎

3.4.1.
Functions, Products, and Sums

Together, the universal property for casts and the
\eta
principles for
each type imply that the casts must behave as in lazy cast semantics:

Theorem 3.15 (Cast Unique Implementation Theorem for
+,\times,\to,\mathbin{\&}
).

The casts’ behavior is uniquely determined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{c}\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle{s}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{case}\,\,s\{x_{1}.\texttt{inl}\,\,{(\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x_{1}})}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{inr}\,\,{(\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{x_{2}})}\}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{rcl}\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\rangle{\bullet}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\texttt{bind}\,\,(s:(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}))\leftarrow\bullet;\texttt{case}\,\,s\\
&&\{{x_{1}^{\prime}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow(\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{x_{1}^{\prime}})});{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{inl}\,\,{x_{1}})}}}\\
&&\mid{x_{2}^{\prime}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow(\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{x_{2}^{\prime}})});{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{inr}\,\,{x_{2}})}}}\}\\
\end{array}\\
\\
\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}\times A_{2}}\rangle{p}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{split}\,\,p\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2}).(\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x_{1}},\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{x_{2}})\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{rcl}\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}\times A_{2})}\rangle{\bullet}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\texttt{bind}\,\,p^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{split}\,\,p^{\prime}\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime}).}\\
&&\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{1}^{\prime}};\\
&&\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{2}^{\prime}};{\texttt{ret}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2})}\\
&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\texttt{bind}\,\,p^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;\texttt{split}\,\,p^{\prime}\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime}).\\
&&\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{2}^{\prime}};\\
&&\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{1}^{\prime}};{\texttt{ret}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2})}\end{array}\\
\\
\langle{\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\pi\mapsto{\langle{\underline{B}_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{\pi\bullet}}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\langle{\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{\pi^{\prime}\bullet}}\}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{rcll}\langle{U(\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2})}\rangle{p}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\texttt{thunk}&{\{\pi\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{1}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})})})}}}\\
&&&\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{2}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi^{\prime}(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})})})}}\}\end{array}\\
\\
\langle{A\to\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\lambda{x}.{\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\bullet\,(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}))}}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{rcll}\langle{U(A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(A\to\underline{B})}\rangle{f}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\lambda x^{\prime}.&\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime})};\\
&&&{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(f)}\,x)})})}})\end{array}\\
\end{array}

In the case for an eager product
\times
, we can actually also show
that reversing the order and running
{\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{2}^{\prime}}}
and then
{\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{1}^{\prime}}}
is also
an implementation of this cast, and therefore equal to the above.
Intuitively, this is sensible because the only effect a downcast
introduces is a run-time error, and if either downcast errors, both
possible implementations will.

Proof.

(1)

Sums upcast. We use Lemma
3.13
with the type
constructor
X_{1}\,\,\text{val type},X_{2}\,\,\text{val type}\vdash X_{1}+X_{2}\,\,\text{val type}
.
Suppose
A_{1}\sqsubseteq A_{1}^{\prime}
and
A_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{2}^{\prime}
and let

s:A_{1}+A_{2}\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{s}:A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}

stand for

\texttt{case}\,\,s\{x_{1}.\texttt{inl}\,\,{(\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x_{1}})}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{inr}\,\,{(\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{x_{2}})}\}

which has the type required for the lemma’s assumption (1).

Assumption (2) requires two condition, both of which are proved by
the congruence rules for
\mathsf{case}
,
\mathsf{inl}
,

\mathsf{inr}
, and upcasts. The first,

s:A_{1}+A_{2}\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A_{1}+A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{s}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{s}:A_{1}+A_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}\\

expands to

\begin{array}[]{c}\texttt{case}\,\,s\{x_{1}.\texttt{inl}\,\,{(\langle{A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x_{1}})}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{inr}\,\,{(\langle{A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{x_{2}})}\}\\
\sqsubseteq\\
\texttt{case}\,\,s\{x_{1}.\texttt{inl}\,\,{(\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x_{1}})}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{inr}\,\,{(\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{x_{2}})}\}\end{array}

The second,

s\sqsubseteq s^{\prime}:A_{1}+A_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{s}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{s^{\prime}}:A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}

expands to

\begin{array}[]{c}\texttt{case}\,\,s\{x_{1}.\texttt{inl}\,\,{(\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x_{1}})}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{inr}\,\,{(\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{x_{2}})}\}\\
\sqsubseteq\\
\texttt{case}\,\,s^{\prime}\{x_{1}.\texttt{inl}\,\,{(\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{x_{1}^{\prime}})}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{inr}\,\,{(\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{x_{2}^{\prime}})}\}\end{array}

Finally, for assumption (3), we need to show

\texttt{case}\,\,s\{x_{1}.\texttt{inl}\,\,{(\langle{A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x_{1}})}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{inr}\,\,{(\langle{A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{x_{2}})}\}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}s

which is true because
\langle{A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle
and
\langle{A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle

are the identity, and using “weak
\eta
” for sums,

\texttt{case}\,\,s\{x_{1}.\texttt{inl}\,\,{x_{1}}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{inr}\,\,{x_{2}}\}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}x
,
which is the special case of the
\eta
rule in
Figure
5
for the identity complex
value:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\texttt{case}\,\,s\{x_{1}.\texttt{inl}\,\,{(\langle{A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x_{1}})}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{inr}\,\,{(\langle{A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{x_{2}})}\}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\\
\texttt{case}\,\,s\{x_{1}.\texttt{inl}\,\,{({x_{1}})}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{inr}\,\,{({x_{2}})}\}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\\
s\end{array}

(2)

Sums downcast. We use the downcast lemma with
X_{1}\,\,\text{val type},X_{2}\,\,\text{val type}\vdash\underline{F}(X_{1}+X_{2})\,\,\text{comp type}
. Let

\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}:\underline{F}(A_{1}+\underline{A}_{2})

stand for

\texttt{bind}\,\,(s:(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}))\leftarrow\bullet;{}{\texttt{case}\,\,s\{x_{1}^{\prime}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow(\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{x_{1}^{\prime}})});{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{inl}\,\,{x_{1}})}}\,\,|\,\,\ldots\}}\\

(where, as in the theorem statement,
\mathsf{inr}
branch is
analogous), which has the correct type for the lemma’s assumption
(1).

For assumption (2), we first need to show

\bullet:{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}:{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\sqsubseteq{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}

i.e.

\begin{array}[]{c}\texttt{bind}\,\,(s^{\prime}:(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}))\leftarrow\bullet;{}{\texttt{case}\,\,s^{\prime}\{x_{1}^{\prime}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow(\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{x_{1}^{\prime}})});{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{inl}\,\,{x_{1}})}}\,\,|\,\,\ldots\}}\\
\sqsubseteq\\
\texttt{bind}\,\,(s^{\prime}:(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}))\leftarrow\bullet;{}{\texttt{case}\,\,s^{\prime}\{x_{1}^{\prime}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}^{\prime}\leftarrow(\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{x_{1}^{\prime}})});{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{inl}\,\,{x_{1}^{\prime}})}}\,\,|\,\,\ldots\}}\end{array}

which is true by the congruence rules for
\mathsf{bind}
,

\mathsf{case}
, downcasts,
\mathsf{ret}
, and
\mathsf{inl}/\mathsf{inr}
.

Next, we need to show

\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\sqsubseteq{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}:{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}

i.e.

\begin{array}[]{c}\texttt{bind}\,\,(s:(A_{1}+A_{2}))\leftarrow\bullet;{}{\texttt{case}\,\,s\{x_{1}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow(\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{x_{1}})});{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{inl}\,\,{x_{1}})}}\,\,|\,\,\ldots\}}\\
\sqsubseteq\\
\texttt{bind}\,\,(s^{\prime}:(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}))\leftarrow\bullet;{}{\texttt{case}\,\,s^{\prime}\{x_{1}^{\prime}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow(\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{x_{1}^{\prime}})});{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{inl}\,\,{x_{1}})}}\,\,|\,\,\ldots\}}\\
\end{array}

which is also true by congruence.

Finally, for assumption (3), we show

\begin{array}[]{lll}\texttt{bind}\,\,(s:(A_{1}+A_{2}))\leftarrow\bullet;{}{\texttt{case}\,\,s\{x_{1}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow(\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,{x_{1}})});{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{inl}\,\,{x_{1}})}}\,\,|\,\,\ldots\}}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\\
\texttt{bind}\,\,(s:(A_{1}+A_{2}))\leftarrow\bullet;{}{\texttt{case}\,\,s\{x_{1}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow({(\texttt{ret}\,\,{x_{1}})});{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{inl}\,\,{x_{1}})}}\,\,|\,\,\ldots\}}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\\
\texttt{bind}\,\,(s:(A_{1}+A_{2}))\leftarrow\bullet;{}{\texttt{case}\,\,s\{x_{1}.{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{inl}\,\,{x_{1}})}}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{inr}\,\,{x_{2}})}}\}}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\\
\texttt{bind}\,\,(s:(A_{1}+A_{2}))\leftarrow\bullet;{}{\texttt{ret}\,\,{s}}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\\
\bullet\end{array}

using the downcast identity,
\beta
for
\underline{F}
types,
\eta
for
sums, and
\eta
for
\underline{F}
types.

(3)

Eager product upcast. We use Lemma
3.13
with the type
constructor
X_{1}\,\,\text{val type},X_{2}\,\,\text{val type}\vdash X_{1}\times X_{2}\,\,\text{val type}
.
Let

p:A_{1}\times A_{2}\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}\times A_{2}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{s}:A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime}

stand for

\texttt{split}\,\,p\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2}).(\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x_{1}},\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{x_{2}})

which has the type required for the lemma’s assumption (1).

Assumption (2) requires two condition, both of which are proved by
the congruence rules for
\mathsf{split}
, pairing, and upcasts.
The first,

p:A_{1}\times A_{2}\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A_{1}\times A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}\times A_{2}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{s}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}\times A_{2}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{s}:A_{1}\times A_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime}\\

expands to

\begin{array}[]{c}\texttt{split}\,\,p\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2}).(\langle{A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x_{1}},\langle{A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{x_{2}})\\
\sqsubseteq\\
\texttt{split}\,\,p\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2}).(\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x_{1}},\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{x_{2}})\\
\end{array}

The second,

p\sqsubseteq p^{\prime}:A_{1}\times A_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime}\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}\times A_{2}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{s}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{s^{\prime}}:A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime}

expands to

\begin{array}[]{c}\texttt{split}\,\,p\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2}).(\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x_{1}},\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{x_{2}})\\
\sqsubseteq\\
\texttt{split}\,\,p^{\prime}\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime}).(\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{x_{1}^{\prime}},\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{x_{2}^{\prime}})\\
\end{array}

Finally, for assumption (3), using
\eta
for products and
the fact that
\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{}
is the identity, we have

\texttt{split}\,\,p\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2}).(\langle{A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x_{1}},\langle{A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{x_{2}})\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{split}\,\,p\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2}).({x_{1}},{x_{2}})\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}p

(4)

Eager product downcast.

We use the downcast lemma with
X_{1}\,\,\text{val type},X_{2}\,\,\text{val type}\vdash\underline{F}(X_{1}\times X_{2})\,\,\text{comp type}
. Let

\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime})\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}\times A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}:\underline{F}(A_{1}\times\underline{A}_{2})

stand for

\texttt{bind}\,\,p^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{split}\,\,p^{\prime}\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime}).\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{1}^{\prime}};{\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{2}^{\prime}};{\texttt{ret}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2})}}}

which has the correct type for the lemma’s assumption (1).

For assumption (2), we first need to show

\bullet:{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime})}\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}\times A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}:{\underline{F}(A_{1}\times A_{2})}\sqsubseteq{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime})}

i.e.

\begin{array}[]{c}\texttt{bind}\,\,p^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{split}\,\,p^{\prime}\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime}).\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{1}^{\prime}};{\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{2}^{\prime}};{\texttt{ret}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2})}}}\\
\sqsubseteq\\
\texttt{bind}\,\,p^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{split}\,\,p^{\prime}\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime}).\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}^{\prime}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{1}^{\prime}};{\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}^{\prime}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{2}^{\prime}};{\texttt{ret}\,\,(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime})}}}\end{array}

which is true by the congruence rules for
\mathsf{bind}
,

\mathsf{split}
, downcasts,
\mathsf{ret}
, and pairing.

Next, we need to show

\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:{\underline{F}(A_{1}\times A_{2})}\sqsubseteq{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime})}\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}\times A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}\times A_{2})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}\times A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}:{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}

i.e.

\begin{array}[]{c}\texttt{bind}\,\,p\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{split}\,\,p\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2}).\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{1}};{\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{2}};{\texttt{ret}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2})}}}\\
\sqsubseteq\\
\texttt{bind}\,\,p^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{split}\,\,p^{\prime}\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime}).\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{1}^{\prime}};{\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{2}^{\prime}};{\texttt{ret}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2})}}}\\
\end{array}

which is also true by congruence.

Finally, for assumption (3), we show

\begin{array}[]{lll}\texttt{bind}\,\,p\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{split}\,\,p\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2}).\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{1}};{\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{2}};{\texttt{ret}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2})}}}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\\
\texttt{bind}\,\,p\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{split}\,\,p\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2}).\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{1}};{\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{2}};{\texttt{ret}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2})}}}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\\
\texttt{bind}\,\,p\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{split}\,\,p\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2}).{\texttt{ret}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2})}}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\\
\texttt{bind}\,\,p\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,p}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\\
\bullet\\
\end{array}

using the downcast identity,
\beta
for
\underline{F}
types,
\eta
for
eager products, and
\eta
for
\underline{F}
types.

An analogous argument works if we sequence the downcasts of the
components in the opposite order:

\texttt{bind}\,\,p^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{split}\,\,p^{\prime}\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime}).\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{2}^{\prime}};{\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{1}^{\prime}};{\texttt{ret}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2})}}}

(the only facts about downcasts used above are congruence and the
downcast identity), which shows that these two implementations of
the downcast are themselves equidynamic.

with
