expands to

\begin{array}[]{c}\{\pi\mapsto{\langle{\underline{B}_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{\pi\bullet^{\prime}}}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\langle{\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{\pi^{\prime}\bullet^{\prime}}}\}\\

\{\pi\mapsto{\langle{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{\pi\bullet^{\prime}}}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\langle{\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{\pi^{\prime}\bullet^{\prime}}}\}\\

The second,

\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}:\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}

expands to

\begin{array}[]{c}\{\pi\mapsto{\langle{\underline{B}_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}}\rangle{\pi\bullet}}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\langle{\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{2}}\rangle{\pi^{\prime}\bullet}}\}\\

\end{array}

For assumption (3), we have, using
\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle
is the

(6)

Lazy product upcast.

We use Lemma
3.13
with the type
constructor
\underline{Y}_{1}\,\,\text{comp type},\underline{Y}_{2}\,\,\text{comp type}\vdash U(\underline{Y}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{Y}_{2})\,\,\text{val type}
.
Let

p:U(\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2})\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{U(\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2})}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{p}:U(\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime})

stand for

\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\{\pi\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{1}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})})})}}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{2}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi^{\prime}(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})})})}}\}}

which has the type required for the lemma’s assumption (1).

Assumption (2) requires two conditions, both of which are proved by
the congruence rules for
\mathsf{thunk}
,
\mathsf{force}
,

p:U(\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2})\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{U(\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{p}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{U(\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{p}:U(\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2})\sqsubseteq U(\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime})

\begin{array}[]{c}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\{\pi\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{1}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})})})}}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{2}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi^{\prime}(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})})})}}\}}\\
\sqsubseteq\\
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\{\pi\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{1}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})})})}}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{2}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi^{\prime}(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})})})}}\}}\end{array}

The second,

p\sqsubseteq p^{\prime}:U(\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2})\sqsubseteq U(\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime})\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{U(\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{p}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{U(\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{p}:U(\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime})

expands to

\begin{array}[]{c}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\{\pi\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{1}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})})})}}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{2}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi^{\prime}(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})})})}}\}}\\
\sqsubseteq\\
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\{\pi\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi(\texttt{force}\,\,{p^{\prime}})})})}}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi^{\prime}(\texttt{force}\,\,{p^{\prime}})})})}}\}}\end{array}

Finally, for assumption (3), using
\eta
for
times
,
\beta
and

\eta
for
U
types, and the fact that
\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{}
is the
identity, we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\{\pi\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{1}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})})})}}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{2}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi^{\prime}(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})})})}}\}}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\\
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\{\pi\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})})}}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\pi^{\prime}(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})})}}\}}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\\
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\{\pi\mapsto{\pi(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\pi^{\prime}(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})}\}}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\\
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{p})}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\\
p\end{array}

(7)

Function downcast.

We use Lemma
3.14
with
the type constructor
X\,\,\text{val type},\underline{Y}\,\,\text{comp type}\vdash X\to\underline{Y}\,\,\text{comp type}
.
Let

\bullet^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A\to\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}:A\to\underline{B}

stand for

\lambda{x}.{\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\bullet\,(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}))}}\\

which has the type required for the lemma’s assumption (1).

Assumption (2) requires two conditions, both of which are proved by
the congruence rules for
\lambda
, application, upcasts, and
downcasts. The first,

\bullet^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{A\to\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}:A\to\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{A}^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}

expands to

\begin{array}[]{c}\lambda{x}.{\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\bullet\,(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}))}}\\
\sqsubseteq\\
\lambda{x^{\prime}}.{\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\bullet\,(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{x^{\prime}}))}}\\
\end{array}

The second,

\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{A}\to\underline{B}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{A\to\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A\to\underline{B}}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{A\to\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}}:A\to\underline{B}

expands to

\begin{array}[]{c}\lambda{x}.{\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle{(\bullet\,(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}))}}\\
\sqsubseteq\\
\lambda{x}.{\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\bullet^{\prime}\,(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}))}}\\
\end{array}

For assumption (3), we have, using
\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle
and
\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle
are the identity and
\eta
for
\to
,

\lambda{x}.{\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle{(\bullet\,(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}))}}\\
\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\lambda{x}.{{(\bullet\,({x}))}}\\
\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\bullet

(8)

Function upcast.

We use Lemma
3.13
with the type
constructor
\underline{X}\,\,\text{val type},\underline{Y}\,\,\text{comp type}\vdash U(\underline{X}\to\underline{Y})\,\,\text{val type}
.
Suppose
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
as value types and
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
as
computation types and let

p:U(A\to\underline{B})\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{U(A\to\underline{B})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(A\to\underline{B})}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{p}:U(A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime})

stand for

\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\lambda x^{\prime}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime})};{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(f)}\,x)})})}})}

which has the type required for the lemma’s assumption (1).

Assumption (2) requires two conditions, both of which are proved by
the congruence rules for
\mathsf{thunk}
,
\mathsf{force}
,
functions, application, upcasts, and downcasts. The first,

f:U(A\to\underline{B})\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{U(A\to\underline{B})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(A\to\underline{B})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{f}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{U(A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(A\to\underline{B})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{f}:U(A\to\underline{B})\sqsubseteq U(A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime})

expands to

\begin{array}[]{c}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\lambda x.\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,x)};{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(f)}\,x)})})}})}\\
\sqsubseteq\\
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\lambda x^{\prime}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime})};{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(f)}\,x)})})}})}\end{array}

The second,

f\sqsubseteq f^{\prime}:U(A\to\underline{B})\sqsubseteq U(A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime})\vdash\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{U(A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(A\to\underline{B})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{f}\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-3.87498pt~\langle{U(A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime})}\rangle\kern-3.87498pt~\rangle{f^{\prime}}:U(A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime})

expands to

\begin{array}[]{c}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\lambda x^{\prime}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime})};{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(f)}\,x)})})}})}\\
\sqsubseteq\\
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\lambda x^{\prime}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime})};{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(f^{\prime})}\,x^{\prime})})})}})}\end{array}

Finally, for assumption (3), using
\eta
for
\to
,
\beta
for
F

types and

\beta/\eta
for
U
types, and the fact that
\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle{}
and

\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A}\rangle
are the identity, we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\lambda x.\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,x)};{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(f)}\,x)})})}})}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\\
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\lambda x.\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{(\texttt{ret}\,\,x)};{\texttt{force}\,\,{{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(f)}\,x)})}}})}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\\
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\lambda x.\texttt{force}\,\,{{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(f)}\,x)})}})}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\\
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\lambda x.(\texttt{force}\,\,{(f)}\,x))}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\\
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(f)})}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\\
f\end{array}

(9)

z:0\vdash\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{0}\rangle z\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{absurd}\,\,z:A
is
immediate by
\eta
for 0 on the map
z:0\vdash\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{0}\rangle z:A
.

∎

3.4.2.
Shifts

In GTT, we assert the existence of value upcasts and computation
downcasts for derivable type dynamism relations. While we do not assert
the existence of all
value
downcasts and
computation

upcasts, we can define the universal property that identifies a term as
such:

Definition 3.16 (Stack upcasts/value downcasts).

(1)

If
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
, a
stack upcast from
B
to
B^{\prime}

is a stack
\bullet:\underline{B}\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle\bullet:\underline{B}^{\prime}
that satisfies the computation dynamism rules of an upcast

{\bullet:\underline{B}\vdash\bullet\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle\bullet:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}}
and

{\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}}
.

(2)

If
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
, a
value downcast from
A^{\prime}
to
A
is a
complex value
x:A^{\prime}\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle x:A
that satisfies
the value dynamism rules of a downcast

{x:A^{\prime}\vdash\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x}\sqsubseteq x:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}}

and

{x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\vdash x\sqsubseteq\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle x^{\prime}:A}
.

Because the proofs of Lemma
3.5
,
Lemma
3.6
, Theorem
3.8
,
Theorem
3.7
rely only on the axioms for
upcasts/downcasts, the analogues of these theorems hold for stack
upcasts and value downcasts as well.
Some value downcasts and computation upcasts do exist, leading to a
characterization of the casts for the monad
U\underline{F}A
and comonad
\underline{F}U\underline{B}
of
F\dashv U
:

Theorem 3.17 (Cast Unique Implementation Theorem for
U\underline{F},\underline{F}U
).

Let
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
and
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
.

(1)

\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,x:A\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})}}:\underline{F}A^{\prime}

is a stack upcast.

(2)

If
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
is a stack upcast, then

x:\underline{U}B\vdash\langle{\underline{U}B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle{x}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x)})}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}

(3)

x:\underline{U}B^{\prime}\vdash\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x)})}:U\underline{B}
is a value downcast.

(4)

If
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
is a value downcast, then

\bullet:\underline{F}A^{\prime}\vdash\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{x})}}

(5)

\begin{array}[]{c}x:U\underline{F}A\vdash\langle{U\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{F}A}\rangle{x}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x:A\leftarrow{\texttt{force}\,\,x};{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})}})}\\
\bullet:\underline{F}U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\langle{\underline{F}U\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x)})})}}\end{array}

Proof.

(1)

To show

\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash\bullet\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,x:A\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})}}:\underline{F}A\sqsubseteq\underline{F}A^{\prime}

we can
\eta
-expand
\bullet\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{x}}
on the left, at which point by
congruence it suffices to show
x\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}
, which
is true up upcast right. To show

\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{F}A\sqsubseteq\underline{F}A^{\prime}\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,x:A\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x})}}\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{F}A^{\prime}

we can
\eta
-expand
\bullet^{\prime}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet^{\prime};{\texttt{ret}\,\,{x^{\prime}}}
on the right,
and then apply congruence, the assumption that
\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}
, and upcast left.

(2)

We apply the upcast lemma with the type constructor
\underline{Y}\,\,\text{comp type}\vdash U\underline{Y}\,\,\text{val type}
. The term
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x)})}
has the correct type for assumption (1). For
assumption (2), we show

x:U\underline{B}\vdash\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x)})}\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x)})}:U\underline{B}\sqsubseteq U\underline{B}^{\prime}

by congruence for
\mathsf{thunk}
,
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
(proved
analogously to Lemma
3.6
), and
\mathsf{force}
.
We show

x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:U\underline{B}\sqsubseteq U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x)})}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x^{\prime})})}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}

by congruence as well.
Finally, for assumption (3), we have

\begin{array}[]{cc}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x)})}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\\
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{({(\texttt{force}\,\,x)})}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\\
x\end{array}

using
\eta
for
U
types and the identity principle for

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
(proved analogously to
Theorem
3.8
).

(3)

To show

x^{\prime}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x^{\prime})})}\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:U\underline{B}\sqsubseteq U\underline{B}^{\prime}

we can
\eta
-expand
x^{\prime}
to
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\texttt{force}\,\,{x^{\prime}}}
, and then by
congruence it suffices to show
\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x^{\prime})}\sqsubseteq\texttt{force}\,\,{x^{\prime}}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
, which is downcast left.
Conversely, for

x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:U\underline{B}\sqsubseteq U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash x\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x^{\prime})})}:U\underline{B}

we
\eta
-expand
x
to
\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{x})}
, and then it suffices
to show
\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{x})}\sqsubseteq\texttt{force}\,\,{x^{\prime}}
, which
is true by downcast right and congruence of
\mathsf{force}
on the
assumption
x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}
.

(4)

We use the downcast lemma with
X\,\,\text{val type}\vdash\underline{F}X\,\,\text{comp type}
,
where
\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x})}}

has the correct type for assumption (1). For assumption (2), we
show

\bullet:\underline{F}A^{\prime}\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x})}}\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet})}}

by congruence for
\mathsf{bind}
,
\mathsf{ret}
, and

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
(which is proved analogously to
Lemma
3.6
).
We also show

\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet^{\prime}:\underline{F}A\sqsubseteq\underline{F}A^{\prime}\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,x:A\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x})}}\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\bullet^{\prime}})}}:\underline{F}A

by congruence.
Finally, for assumption (3), we have

\begin{array}[]{rc}\texttt{bind}\,\,x:A\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x})}}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\\
\texttt{bind}\,\,x:A\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{({x})}}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\\
\bullet\end{array}

using the identity principle for
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
(proved
analogously to Theorem
3.8
) and
\eta
for
F

types.

(5)

Combining parts (1) and (2) gives the first equation, while
combining parts (3) and (4) gives the second equation.

∎

3.4.3.
Derived Rules for Call-by-value Function Types

Recall that for value types
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
, the CBV function type is

U(A_{1}\to\underline{F}A_{2})
. As a corollary of
Theorems
3.15
and

3.17
, we have

Corollary 3.18 (Cast Unique Implementation for CBV Functions).

\small\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{rcll}\langle{U(A_{1}^{\prime}\to\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(A_{1}\to\underline{F}A_{2})}\rangle{f}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\lambda x^{\prime}.&\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime})};\\
&&&\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow(\texttt{force}\,\,{(f)}\,x);\\
&&&{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{y})}})\\
\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{rcl}\langle{\underline{F}U(A_{1}\to\underline{F}A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}U(A_{1}^{\prime}\to\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle{\bullet}&\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}&\texttt{bind}\,\,f\leftarrow\bullet;\\
&&{\texttt{ret}\,\,{\lambda{x}.{\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(f)}\,(\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x}))}}}}\end{array}\end{array}

Proof.

For the upcast, by Theorem
3.15
, it’s equal to

\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\lambda x^{\prime}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{(\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime})};{}{\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{F}A_{2}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(f)}\,x)})})}})

By Theorem
3.17
,
\langle{U\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{F}A_{2}}\rangle
is equal to

\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{\texttt{force}\,\,-};{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{x})}})}

so
\beta
-reducing
force
and
thunk
twice gives the result.

For the downcast, by Theorem
3.17
, it’s
equal to

\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle{\underline{(}A_{1}\to\underline{F}A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{(}A_{1}\to\underline{F}A_{2})}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x)})})}}

and by Theorem
3.15

\langle{\underline{(}A_{1}\to\underline{F}A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{(}A_{1}\to\underline{F}A_{2})}\rangle{-}
is equal to

\lambda{x}.{\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle{(-\,(\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x}))}}

∎

These are equivalent to the CBPV translations of the standard CBV wrapping
implementations; for example, the CBV upcast term

\lambda x^{\prime}.\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle{x^{\prime}};{\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle{(fx^{\prime})}}

has its evaluation order made explicit, and the fact that its upcast is
a (complex) value exposed. In the downcast, the GTT term is free to
let-bind
(\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle{x})
to avoid duplicating it, but because
it is a (complex) value, it can also be substituted directly, which
might expose reductions that can be optimized.

3.5.
Least Dynamic Types

Theorem 3.19 (Least Dynamic Value Type).

If
\bot_{\mathsf{v}}
is a type such that
\bot_{\mathsf{v}}\sqsubseteq A
for all
A
,
then in GTT with a strict initial object
0
,
\bot_{\mathsf{v}}\cong_{v}0
.

Proof.

We have the upcast
x:\bot_{\mathsf{v}}\vdash\langle{0}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\bot_{\mathsf{v}}}\rangle{x}:0
, so Lemma
3.2
gives the result.
∎

The fact that
\bot_{\mathsf{v}}
is strictly initial seems to depend on the
fact that we have a strictly initial object: In GTT without a
0
type,
it seems that we cannot prove that
x:\bot_{\mathsf{v}}\vdash\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\bot_{\mathsf{v}}}\rangle{x}:A
is the unique such map.

Theorem 3.20 (Least Dynamic Computation Type).

If
\bot_{\mathsf{c}}
is a type such that
\bot_{\mathsf{c}}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}
for all
\underline{B}
, and we have a terminal computation type
\top
, then
U\bot_{\mathsf{c}}\cong_{v}U\top
.

Proof.

We have stacks
\bullet:\top\langle{\bot_{\mathsf{c}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\top}\rangle{\bullet}:\bot_{\mathsf{c}}
and
\bullet:\bot_{\mathsf{c}}\vdash\{\}:\top
. The
composite at
\top
is the identity by Lemma
3.3
. However,
because
\top
is not a strict terminal object, the dual of the above
argument does not give a stack isomorphism
\bot_{\mathsf{c}}\cong_{c}\top
.

However, using the retract axiom, we have

\begin{array}[]{c}x:U\bot_{\mathsf{c}}\vdash\langle{U\top}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\bot_{\mathsf{c}}}\rangle{x}:U\top\\
y:U\top\vdash\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle{\bot_{\mathsf{c}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\top}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{x})})}:U\bot_{\mathsf{c}}\\
x:U\bot_{\mathsf{c}}\vdash\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle{\bot_{\mathsf{c}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\top}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\top}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\bot_{\mathsf{c}}}\rangle{x})})})}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}x:U\bot_{\mathsf{c}}\end{array}

and the composite

y:U\top\vdash\langle{U\top}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\bot_{\mathsf{c}}}\rangle{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle{\bot_{\mathsf{c}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\top}\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,{x})})})}:U\top

is the identity by uniqueness for
U\top
(Lemma
3.3
).
∎

This suggests taking
\bot_{v}:=0
and
\bot_{c}:=\top
.

Theorem 3.21.

The casts determined by
0\sqsubseteq A
are

\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{0}\rangle z\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{absurd}\,\,z\qquad\langle{\underline{F}0}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A}\rangle{\bullet}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,\_\leftarrow\bullet;{\mho}

Dually, the casts determined by
\top\sqsubseteq\underline{B}
are

\langle{\top}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle{\bullet}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\}\qquad\langle{U\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\top}\rangle{u}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,\mho

Proof.

(1)

x:0\vdash\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{0}\rangle{x}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{abort}\,\,{x}:A
is
immediate by
\eta
for
0
.

(2)

First, to show

\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,\_\leftarrow\bullet;{\mho}\sqsubseteq\langle{\underline{F}0}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A}\rangle{\bullet}
,
we can
\eta
-expand the right-hand side into

\texttt{bind}\,\,x:A\leftarrow\bullet;{\langle{\underline{F}0}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,{x}}}
,
at which point the result follows by congruence
and the fact that type error is minimal, so

\mho\sqsubseteq{\langle{\underline{F}0}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A}\rangle{\texttt{ret}\,\,{x}}}
.

Second, to show

\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash\langle{\underline{F}0}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A}\rangle{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,\_\leftarrow\bullet;{\mho}
,
we can
\eta
-expand the left-hand side to

\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}0}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A}\rangle{\bullet};{\texttt{ret}\,\,y}
,
so we need to show

\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,y:0\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}0}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A}\rangle{\bullet};{\texttt{ret}\,\,y}\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,y^{\prime}:A\leftarrow\bullet;{\mho}:\underline{F}0

We apply congruence, with
\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash{\langle{\underline{F}0}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A}\rangle{\bullet}}\sqsubseteq\bullet:0\sqsubseteq A
by the
universal property of downcasts in the first premise, so it suffices
to show

y\sqsubseteq y^{\prime}:0\sqsubseteq A\vdash\texttt{ret}\,\,{y}\sqsubseteq\mho_{\underline{F}0}:\underline{F}0

By transitivity with
y\sqsubseteq y^{\prime}:0\sqsubseteq A\vdash\mho_{\underline{F}0}\sqsubseteq\mho_{\underline{F}0}:\underline{F}0\sqsubseteq\underline{F}0
, it suffices to show

y\sqsubseteq y:0\sqsubseteq 0\vdash\texttt{ret}\,\,{y}\sqsubseteq\mho_{\underline{F}0}:\underline{F}0

But now both sides are maps out of
0
, and therefore equal by
Lemma
3.2
.

(3)

The downcast is immediate by
\eta
for
\top
,
Lemma
3.3
.

(4)

First,

u:U\top\vdash\texttt{thunk}\,\,\mho\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{force}\,\,{(\langle{U\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\top}\rangle{u})})}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}{\langle{U\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\top}\rangle{u}}:U\underline{B}

by congruence,
\eta
for
U
, and the fact that error is minimal.
Conversely, to show

u:U\top\vdash{\langle{U\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\top}\rangle{u}}\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,\mho:U\underline{B}

it suffices to show

u:U\top\vdash u\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,\mho_{\underline{B}}:U\top\sqsubseteq U\underline{B}

by the universal property of an upcast. By Lemma
3.3
,
any two elements of
U\top
are equidynamic, so in particular
u\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\mho_{\top}}
, at which point congruence for

\mathsf{thunk}
and
\mho_{\top}\sqsubseteq\mho_{\underline{B}}:\top\sqsubseteq\underline{B}
gives the result.

∎

3.6.
Upcasts are Values, Downcasts are Stacks

Since GTT is an axiomatic theory, we can consider different fragments
than the one presented in Section
2
. Here, we use this
flexibility to show that taking upcasts to be complex values and
downcasts to be complex stacks is forced if this property holds for
casts between
ground
types and
{?}
/
\underline{\text{?`}}
. For this section, we define a
ground
type
3
3

3

In gradual
typing, “ground” is used to mean a one-level unrolling of a dynamic type, not first-order data.
to be generated by the following grammar:

G::=1\mid{?}\times{?}\mid 0\mid{?}+{?}\mid U\underline{\text{?`}}\qquad\underline{G}::={?}\to\underline{\text{?`}}\mid\top\mid\underline{\text{?`}}\mathbin{\&}\underline{\text{?`}}\mid\underline{F}{?}

Definition 3.22 (Ground type dynamism).

Let
A\sqsubseteq^{\prime}A^{\prime}
and
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq^{\prime}\underline{B}^{\prime}
be the relations defined
by the rules in Figure
2

with the axioms
A\sqsubseteq{?}
and
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{\text{?`}}
restricted to
ground types—i.e., replaced by
G\sqsubseteq{?}
and
\underline{G}\sqsubseteq\underline{\text{?`}}
.

Lemma 3.23.

For any type
A
,
A\sqsubseteq^{\prime}{?}
.
For any type
\underline{B}
,
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq^{\prime}\underline{\text{?`}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the type. For example, in the case for
A_{1}+A_{2}
, we
have by the inductive hypothesis
A_{1}\sqsubseteq^{\prime}{?}
and
A_{2}\sqsubseteq^{\prime}{?}
, so
A_{1}+A_{2}\sqsubseteq^{\prime}{?}+{?}\sqsubseteq{?}
by congruence
and transitivity, because
{?}+{?}
is ground. In the case for

\underline{F}A
, we have
A\sqsubseteq{?}
by the inductive hypothesis, so
\underline{F}A\sqsubseteq\underline{F}{?}\sqsubseteq\underline{\text{?`}}
.
∎

Lemma 3.24 (
\sqsubseteq
and
\sqsubseteq^{\prime}
agree).

A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
iff
A\sqsubseteq^{\prime}A^{\prime}
and
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
iff
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq^{\prime}\underline{B}^{\prime}

Proof.

The “if” direction is immediate by induction because every rule of

\sqsubseteq^{\prime}
is a rule of
\sqsubseteq
. To show
\sqsubseteq
is contained in

\sqsubseteq^{\prime}
, we do induction on the derivation of
\sqsubseteq
, where every
rule is true for
\sqsubseteq^{\prime}
, except
A\sqsubseteq{?}
and
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{\text{?`}}
, and for these, we use Lemma
3.23
.
∎

Let GTT
G
be the fragment of GTT where the only primitive casts are
those between ground types and the dynamic types, i.e. the cast terms
are restricted to the substitution closures of

\small\begin{array}[]{llll}x:G\vdash\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{G}\rangle{x}:{?}&\bullet:\underline{F}{?}\vdash\langle{\underline{F}G}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}{?}}\rangle{\bullet}:\underline{F}{?}&\bullet:\underline{\text{?`}}\vdash\langle{\underline{G}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle{\bullet}:\underline{\text{?`}}&x:U\underline{G}\vdash\langle{U\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{G}}\rangle{x}:U\underline{\text{?`}}\end{array}

Lemma 3.25 (Casts are Admissible).

In GTT
G
it is admissible that

(1)

for all
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}

there is a complex value
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle

satisfying the universal property of an upcast
and a complex stack
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle

satisfying the universal property of a downcast

(2)

for all
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
there is a complex
stack
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
satisfying the universal property of a
downcast and a complex value
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
satisfying
the universal property of an upcast.

Proof.

To streamline the exposition above, we stated
Theorems
3.8
, Theorem
3.15

Theorem
3.17
as showing that the
“definitions” of each cast are equidynamic with the cast that is a
priori postulated to exist (e.g.
\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle}
). However, the proofs
factor
through Theorem
3.7
and Lemma
3.13
and
Lemma
3.14
, which
show directly that the right-hand sides have the desired universal
property—i.e. the stipulation that some cast with the correct
universal property exists is not used in the proof that the
implementation has the desired universal property. Moreover, the
proofs given do not rely on any axioms of GTT besides the universal
properties of the “smaller” casts used in the definition and the

\beta\eta
rules for the relevant types. So these proofs can be used
as the inductive steps here, in GTT
G
.

By induction on type dynamism
A\sqsubseteq^{\prime}A^{\prime}
and
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq^{\prime}\underline{B}^{\prime}
.

(We chose not to make this more explicit above, because we believe the
equational description in a language with all casts is a clearer
description of the results, because it avoids needing to hypothesize
terms that behave as the smaller casts in each case.)

We show a few representative cases:

In the cases for
G\sqsubseteq{?}
or
\underline{G}\sqsubseteq\underline{\text{?`}}
, we have
assumed appropriate casts
\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{G}\rangle
and

\langle{\underline{F}G}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}{?}}\rangle
and

\langle{\underline{G}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle
and

\langle{U\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{G}}\rangle
.

In the case for identity
A\sqsubseteq A
, we need to show that there is
an upcast
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
and a downcast
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle

The proof of Theorem
3.8
shows that the identity
value and stack have the correct universal property.

In the case where type dynamism was concluded by
transitivity between
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
and
A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}
, by the
inductive hypotheses we get upcasts
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
and

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
, and the proof of
Theorem
3.8
shows that defining

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
to be
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle}

has the correct universal property. For the downcast, we get

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
and

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
by the inductive hypotheses, and the
proof of Theorem
3.8
shows that their composition
has the correct universal property.

In the case where type dynamism was concluded by the congruence rule
for
A_{1}+A_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}
from
A_{i}\sqsubseteq A_{i}^{\prime}
, we have
upcasts
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A_{i}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{i}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
and downcasts
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{F}A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{i}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
by the inductive hypothesis, and the proof of
Theorem
3.8
shows that the definitions given there
have the desired universal property.

In the case where type dynamism was concluded by the congruence rule
for
\underline{F}A\sqsubseteq\underline{F}A^{\prime}
from
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
, we obtain by induction
an
upcast

A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
and a downcast
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
.
We need a

downcast

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{FA^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
, which we have,
and an
upcast

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{U\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{U\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
, which is
constructed as in Theorem
3.17
.
∎

As discussed in Section
2.4
, rather than an upcast being
a complex value
x:A\vdash\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}:A^{\prime}
, an a priori more
general type would be a stack
\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash\langle{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{F}A}\rangle{\bullet}:\underline{F}A^{\prime}
, which allows the upcast to perform
effects; dually, an a priori more general type for a downcast
\bullet:\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet}:\underline{B}
would be a value
x:U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\langle{U\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{x}:U\underline{B}
, which allows the
downcast to ignore its argument. The following shows that in GTT
G
,
if we postulate such stack upcasts/value downcasts as originally
suggested in Section
2.4
, then in fact these casts

must
be equal to the action of
U
/
\underline{F}
on some
value upcasts/stack downcasts, so the potential
for (co)effectfulness affords no additional flexibility.

Theorem 3.26 (Upcasts are Necessarily Values, Downcasts are Necessarily Stacks).

Suppose we extend GTT
G
with the following postulated stack upcasts
and value downcasts (in the sense of
Definition
3.16
): For every type
precision
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
, there is a stack upcast
\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash\langle{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{F}A}\rangle{\bullet}:\underline{F}A^{\prime}
, and for every
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
, there is a complex value downcast
x:U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\langle{U\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{x}:U\underline{B}
.

Then there exists a value upcast
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
and a stack
downcast
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
such that

\begin{array}[]{c}\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash\langle{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{F}A}\rangle{\bullet}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x:A\leftarrow{\bullet};{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x})}})}\\
x:U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\langle{U\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{x}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x)})})}\end{array}

Proof.

Lemma
3.25
constructs
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
and

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
, so the proof of
Theorem
3.17
(which really works for any

\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
and
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
with the correct
universal properties, not only the postulated casts) implies that the
right-hand sides of the above equations are stack upcasts and value
downcasts of the appropriate type. Since stack upcasts/value downcasts
are unique by an argument analogous to Theorem
3.7
,
the postulated casts must be equal to these.
∎

Indeed, the following a priori even more general assumption provides no
more flexibility:

Theorem 3.27 (Upcasts are Necessarily Values, Downcasts are Necessarily Stacks II).

Suppose we extend GTT
G
only with postulated monadic upcasts
x:U\underline{F}A\vdash\langle{U\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{F}A}\rangle{x}:U\underline{F}A^{\prime}
for every

A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
and comonadic downcasts
\bullet:\underline{F}U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\langle{\underline{F}U\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet}:\underline{F}U\underline{B}
for every

\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
.

Then there exists a value upcast
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
and a stack
downcast
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
such that

\begin{array}[]{c}x:U\underline{F}A\vdash\langle{U\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{F}A}\rangle{x}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x:A\leftarrow{\texttt{force}\,\,x};{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{x})}})}\\
\bullet:\underline{F}U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\langle{\underline{F}U\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;{\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle{(\texttt{force}\,\,x)})})}}\end{array}

In CBV terms, the monadic upcast is like an upcast from
A
to
A^{\prime}

taking having type
(1\to A)\to A^{\prime}
, i.e. it takes a thunked
effectful computation of an
A
as input and produces an effectful
computation of an
A^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Again, Lemma
3.25
constructs
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle

and
\langle\kern-4.30554pt~\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\kern-4.30554pt~\rangle
, so the proof of part (5) of
Theorem
3.17
gives the result.
∎

3.7.
Equidynamic Types are Isomorphic

Theorem 3.28 (Equidynamism implies Isomorphism).

(1)

If
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
and
A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A
then
A\cong_{v}A^{\prime}
.

(2)

If
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
and
\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}
then
\underline{B}\cong_{c}\underline{B}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

(1)

We have upcasts
x:A\vdash\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}:A^{\prime}
and
x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\vdash\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{x^{\prime}}:A
.
For the composites, to show

x:A\vdash\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}}\sqsubseteq x

we apply upcast left twice, and conclude
x\sqsubseteq x
by assumption.
To show,

x:A\vdash x\sqsubseteq\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}}
,
we have
x:A\vdash x\sqsubseteq{\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}

by upcast right, and therefore

x:A\vdash x\sqsubseteq\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{x}}:A\sqsubseteq A

again by upcast right.
The other composite is the same proof with
A
and
A^{\prime}
swapped.

(2)

We have downcasts
\bullet:\underline{B}\vdash\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet}:\underline{B}^{\prime}
and

\bullet:\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle{\bullet}:\underline{B}
.

For the composites, to show
\bullet:\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\bullet\sqsubseteq\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle{\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle}{\bullet}
, we apply
downcast right twice, and conclude
\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet
. For

\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle{\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle}{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\bullet
, we first have
\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle{\bullet}\sqsubseteq\bullet:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
by downcast left, and then the result
by another application of downcast left.
The other composite is the same proof with
\underline{B}
and
\underline{B}^{\prime}
swapped.

∎

4.
Contract Models of GTT

To show the soundness of our theory, and demonstrate its relationship to
operational definitions of observational equivalence and the gradual
guarantee, we develop
models
of GTT using observational error
approximation of a
non-gradual
CBPV.
We call this the
contract translation
because it translates the
built-in casts of the gradual language into ordinary terms implemented
in a non-gradual language.
While contracts are typically implemented in a dynamically typed
language, our target is typed, retaining type information similarly to
manifest contracts
(
Greenberg
et al. 2010
)
.
We give implementations of the dynamic value type in the usual way as
a recursive sum of basic value types, i.e., using type tags, and we
give implementations of the dynamic computation type as the dual: a
recursive product of basic computation types.

Writing
\llbracket M\rrbracket
for any of the contract translations, the remaining
sections of the paper establish:

Theorem 4.1 (Equi-dynamism implies Observational Equivalence).

If
\Gamma\vdash M_{1}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}M_{2}:\underline{B}
, then for any closing
GTT context
C:(\Gamma\vdash\underline{B})\Rightarrow(\cdot\vdash\underline{F}(1+1))
,
\llbracket C[M_{1}]\rrbracket
and
\llbracket C[M_{2}]\rrbracket
have the same behavior: both diverge,
both run to an error, or both run to
true
or both run to
false
.

Theorem 4.2 (Graduality).

If
\Gamma_{1}\sqsubseteq\Gamma_{2}\vdash M_{1}\sqsubseteq M_{2}:B_{1}\sqsubseteq B_{2}
,
then for any GTT context
C:(\Gamma_{1}\vdash B_{1})\Rightarrow(\cdot\vdash\underline{F}(1+1))
, and any valid interpretation of the dynamic
types, either

(1)

\llbracket C[M_{1}]\rrbracket\Downarrow\mho
, or

(2)

\llbracket C[M_{1}]\rrbracket\Uparrow
and
\llbracket C[\langle{B_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{2}}\rangle M_{2}[\langle{\Gamma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\Gamma_{1}}\rangle{\Gamma_{1}}]]\rrbracket\Uparrow
, or

(3)

\llbracket C[M_{1}]\rrbracket\Downarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,V
,

\llbracket C[\langle{B_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{2}}\rangle M_{2}[\langle{\Gamma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\Gamma_{1}}\rangle{\Gamma_{1}}]]\rrbracket\Downarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,V
, and
V=\texttt{true}
or
V=\texttt{false}
.

As a consequence we will also get consistency of our logic of
dynamism:

Corollary 4.3 (Consistency of GTT ).

\cdot\vdash\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{true}\,\,\sqsubseteq\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{false}\,\,:\underline{F}(1+1)
is not
provable in GTT.

Proof.

They are distinguished by the identity context.∎

We break down this proof into 3 major steps.

(1)

(This section) We translate GTT into a statically typed CBPV*
language where the casts of GTT are translated to “contracts” in
GTT: i.e., CBPV terms that implement the runtime type checking. We
translate the term dynamism of GTT to an inequational theory for CBPV.
Our translation is parameterized by the implementation of the dynamic
types, and we demonstrate two valid implementations, one more direct
and one more Scheme-like.

(2)

(Section
5
) Next, we eliminate all uses of complex
values and stacks from the CBPV language. We translate the complex
values and stacks to terms with a proof that they are “pure”
(thunkable or linear
(
Munch-Maccagnoni 2014
)
). This part has little to do with GTT
specifically, except that it shows the behavioral property that
corresponds to upcasts being complex values and downcasts being
complex stacks.

(3)

(Section
6.3
) Finally, with complex values and stacks
eliminated, we give a standard operational semantics for CBPV and
define a
logical relation
that is sound and complete with
respect to observational error approximation. Using the logical
relation, we show that the inequational theory of CBPV is sound for
observational error approximation.

By composing these, we get a model of GTT where equidynamism is sound
for observational equivalence and an operational semantics that
satisfies the graduality theorem.

4.1.
Call-by-push-value

Next, we define the call-by-push-value language CBPV* that will be
the target for our contract translations of GTT.
CBPV* is the axiomatic version of call-by-push-value
with

complex values and stacks, while CBPV (Section
5
) will
designate the operational version of call-by-push-value with only
operational values and stacks.
CBPV* is almost a subset of GTT obtained as follows: We remove the
casts and the dynamic types
{?},\underline{\text{?`}}
(the shaded pieces) from the
syntax and typing rules in Figure
1
. There
is no type dynamism, and the inequational theory of CBPV* is the
homogeneous fragment of term dynamism in
Figure
3
and Figure
4
(judgements
\Gamma\vdash E\sqsubseteq E^{\prime}:T
where
\Gamma\vdash E,E^{\prime}:T
, with all the same rules
in that figure thus restricted). The inequational axioms are the
Type Universal Properties (
\beta\eta
rules)
and Error Properties (with
ErrBot
made homogeneous) from
Figure
5
.
To implement the casts and dynamic types, we
add
general

recursive
value types (
\mu X.A
, the fixed point of
X\,\,\text{val type}\vdash A\,\,\text{val type}
) and
corecursive
computation types (
\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}
, the fixed point of
\underline{Y}\,\,\text{comp type}\vdash\underline{B}\,\,\text{comp type}
).
The recursive type
\mu X.A
is a value type with constructor

roll
, whose eliminator is pattern matching, whereas the
corecursive type
\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}
is a computation type defined by its
eliminator (
unroll
), with an introduction form that we also write
as
roll
.
We extend the inequational theory with monotonicity of each term constructor of
the recursive types, and with their
\beta\eta
rules.

In the following figure, we write
\mathrel{\bf+::=}
and
\mathrel{\bf-::=}
to indicate
the diff from the grammar in Figure
1
.

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}\text{Value Types}&A&\mathrel{\bf+::=}&\mu X.A\mathrel{\bf\,\mid\,}X\\
&&\mathrel{\bf-::=}&{?}\\
\text{Computation Types}&\underline{B}&\mathrel{\bf+::=}&\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}\mathrel{\bf\,\mid\,}\underline{Y}\\
&&\mathrel{\bf-::=}&\underline{\text{?`}}\\
\text{Values}&V&\mathrel{\bf+::=}&\texttt{roll}_{\mu X.A}\,\,V\\
&&\mathrel{\bf-::=}&\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle V\\
\text{Terms}&M&\mathrel{\bf+::=}&\texttt{roll}_{\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}}\,\,M\mathrel{\bf\,\mid\,}\texttt{unroll}\,\,M\\
&M&\mathrel{\bf-::=}&\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle M\\
\text{Both}&E&\mathrel{\bf+::=}&\texttt{unroll}\,\,V\,\texttt{to}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,x.E\end{array}

\frac{\Gamma\vdash V:A[\mu X.A/X]}{\Gamma\vdash\texttt{roll}_{\mu X.A}\,\,V:\mu X.A}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=$\mu$I}\hskip 18.49988pt\frac{\Gamma\vdash V:\mu X.A\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma,x:A[\mu X.A/X]\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash E:T}{\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash\texttt{unroll}\,\,V\,\texttt{to}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,x.E:T}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=$\mu$E}\par\frac{\Gamma\mid\Delta\vdash M:\underline{B}[\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}]}{\Gamma\mid\Delta\vdash\texttt{roll}_{\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}}\,\,M:\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=$\nu$I}\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\frac{\Gamma\mid\Delta\vdash M:\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}}{\Gamma\mid\Delta\vdash\texttt{unroll}\,\,M:\underline{B}[\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}]}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=$\nu$E}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash V\sqsubseteq V^{\prime}:A[\mu X.A/X]}{\Gamma\vdash\texttt{roll}\,\,V\sqsubseteq\texttt{roll}\,\,V^{\prime}:\mu X.A}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=$\mu$ICong}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash V\sqsubseteq V^{\prime}:\mu X.A\Gamma,x:A[\mu X.A/X]\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash E\sqsubseteq E^{\prime}:T}{\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash\texttt{unroll}\,\,V\,\texttt{to}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,x.E\sqsubseteq\texttt{unroll}\,\,V^{\prime}\,\texttt{to}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,x.E^{\prime}:T}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=$\mu$ECong}\par\frac{\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash M\sqsubseteq M^{\prime}:\underline{B}[\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}/\underline{Y}]}{\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash\texttt{roll}\,\,M\sqsubseteq\texttt{roll}\,\,M^{\prime}:\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=$\nu$ICong}\par\frac{\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash M\sqsubseteq M^{\prime}:\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}}{\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash\texttt{unroll}\,\,M\sqsubseteq\texttt{unroll}\,\,M^{\prime}:\underline{B}[\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}/\underline{Y}]}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=$\nu$ECong}\\
\framebox{Recursive Type Axioms}\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt

Type

\beta

\eta

\mu

{\texttt{unroll}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,V\,\texttt{to}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,x.E\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}E[V/x]}

\begin{array}[]{l}E\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{unroll}\,\,x\,\texttt{to}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,y.E[\texttt{roll}\,\,y/x]\\
\text{where }{x:\mu X.A\vdash E:T}\end{array}

\nu

{\texttt{unroll}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}M}
