full rules in the extended version

4.2.
Interpreting the Dynamic Types

As shown in Theorems
3.8
,

3.15
,
3.17
, almost
all of the contract translation is uniquely determined already.
However, the interpretation of the dynamic types and the casts between
the dynamic types and ground types
G
and
\underline{G}
are not determined
(they were still postulated in Lemma
3.25
).
For this reason, our translation is
parameterized
by an
interpretation of the dynamic types and the ground casts.
By Theorems
3.9
,
3.10
, we know
that these must be
embedding-projection pairs
(ep pairs), which
we now define in CBPV*.
There are two kinds of ep pairs we consider: those between value types
(where the embedding models an upcast) and those between computation
types (where the projection models a downcast).

Definition 4.4 (Value and Computation Embedding-Projection Pairs).

(1)

A
value ep pair
from
A
to
A^{\prime}
consists of
an
embedding
value
x:A\vdash V_{e}:A^{\prime}

and
projection
stack
\bullet:\underline{F}A^{\prime}\vdash S_{p}:\underline{F}A
,
satisfying the
retraction
and
projection
properties:

x:A\vdash\texttt{ret}\,\,x\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}S_{p}[\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e}]:\underline{F}A\qquad\bullet:\underline{F}A^{\prime}\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow S_{p};\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e}\sqsubseteq\bullet:\underline{F}A^{\prime}

(2)

A
computation ep pair
from
\underline{B}
to
\underline{B}^{\prime}
consists of
an
embedding
value
z:U\underline{B}\vdash V_{e}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}

and a
projection
stack
\bullet:\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash S_{p}:\underline{B}

satisfying
retraction
and
projection
properties:

z:U\underline{B}\vdash\texttt{force}\,\,z\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,V_{e}]:\underline{B}\qquad w:U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash V_{e}[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,w]}]\sqsubseteq w:U\underline{B}^{\prime}

While this formulation is very convenient in that both kinds of ep
pairs are pairs of a value and a stack, the projection properties are
often occur more naturally in the following forms:

Lemma 4.5 (Alternative Projection).

If
(V_{e},S_{p})
is a value ep pair from
A
to
A^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,y:A^{\prime}\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash M:\underline{B}
, then

\Gamma,x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow S_{p}[\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime}];M[V_{e}/y]\sqsubseteq M[x^{\prime}/y]

Similarly, if
(V_{e},S_{p})
is a computation ep pair from
\underline{B}
to

\underline{B}^{\prime}
, and
\Gamma\vdash M:\underline{B}^{\prime}
then

\Gamma\vdash V_{e}[\texttt{thunk}\,\,S_{p}[M]]\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,M:U\underline{B}^{\prime}

Proof.

For the first,

(comm conv,
\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow S_{p}[\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime}];M[V_{e}/y]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow S_{p}[\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime}];\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e});M

(projection)

\displaystyle\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime};M

(
\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle M[x^{\prime}/y]

For the second,

(
U\beta
)

\displaystyle V_{e}[\texttt{thunk}\,\,S_{p}[M]]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}V_{e}[\texttt{thunk}\,\,S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M]]

(projection)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,M

∎

Using this, and using the notion of ground type from
Section
3.6

with
0
and
\top
removed
, we define

Definition 4.6 (Dynamic Type Interpretation).

A
{?},\underline{\text{?`}}
interpretation
\rho
consists of (1) a
CBPV value type
\rho({?})
, (2) a CBPV computation
type
\rho(\underline{\text{?`}})
,
(3)
for each value ground type
G
,
a value ep pair
(x.\rho_{e}(G),\rho_{p}(G))
from
\llbracket G\rrbracket_{\rho}
to

\rho({?})
, and (4) for each computation ground type
\underline{G}
, a
computation ep pair
(z.\rho_{e}(\underline{G}),\rho_{p}(\underline{G}))
from

\llbracket\underline{G}\rrbracket_{\rho}
to
\rho(\underline{\text{?`}})
. We write

\llbracket G\rrbracket_{\rho}
and
\llbracket\underline{G}\rrbracket_{\rho}
for the interpretation of a ground type,
replacing
{?}
with
\rho({?})
,
\underline{\text{?`}}
with
\rho(\underline{\text{?`}})
, and
compositionally otherwise.

Next, we show several possible interpretations of the dynamic type
that will all give, by construction, implementations that satisfy the
gradual guarantee.
Our interpretations of the value dynamic type are not surprising.
They are the usual construction of the dynamic type using type tags:
i.e., a recursive sum of basic value types.
On the other hand, our interpretations of the computation dynamic type
are less familiar.
In duality with the interpretation of
{?}
, we interpret
\underline{\text{?`}}
as
a recursive
product
of basic computation types.
This interpretation has some analogues in previous work on the duality
of computation
(
Girard 2001
;
Zeilberger 2009
)
, but the
most direct interpretation (definition
4.10
)
does not correspond to any known work on dynamic/gradual typing.
Then we show that a particular choice of which computation types is
basic and which are derived produces an interpretation of the dynamic
computation type as a type of variable-arity functions whose arguments
are passed on the stack, producing a model similar to Scheme without
accounting for control effects (definition

4.15
).

4.2.1.
Natural Dynamic Type Interpretation

Our first dynamic type interpretation is to make the value and
computation dynamic types sums and products of the ground value and
computation types, respectively.
This forms a model of GTT for the following reasons.
For the value dynamic type
{?}
, we need a value embedding (the
upcast) from each ground value type
G
with a corresponding projection.
The easiest way to do this would be if for each
G
, we could rewrite

{?}
as a sum of the values that fit
G
and those that don’t:

{?}\cong G+{?}_{-G}
because of the following lemma.

Lemma 4.7 (Sum Injections are Value Embeddings).

For any
A,A^{\prime}
, there are value ep pairs from
A
and
A^{\prime}
to

A+A^{\prime}
where the embeddings are
inl
and
inr
.

Proof.

Define the embedding of
A
to just be
x.\texttt{inl}\,\,x
and the
projection to be
\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\bullet;\texttt{case}\,\,y\{\texttt{inl}\,\,x.\texttt{ret}\,\,x\,\,|\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,_{.}\mho\}
.
This satisfies retraction (using
\underline{F}(+)
induction (lemma
4.8
),
inr
case is the same):

(
\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\texttt{inl}\,\,x;\texttt{case}\,\,y\{\texttt{inl}\,\,x.\texttt{ret}\,\,x\,\,|\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,_{.}\mho\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{case}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,x\{\texttt{inl}\,\,x.\texttt{ret}\,\,x\,\,|\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,_{.}\mho\}

(
+\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{ret}\,\,x

and projection (similarly using
\underline{F}(+)
induction):

\displaystyle x^{\prime}:A+A^{\prime}

\displaystyle\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,(\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime};\texttt{case}\,\,y\{\texttt{inl}\,\,x.\texttt{ret}\,\,x\,\,|\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,_{.}\mho\})\leftarrow x;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,x

(
\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,(\texttt{case}\,\,x^{\prime}\{\texttt{inl}\,\,x.\texttt{ret}\,\,x\,\,|\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,_{.}\mho\})\leftarrow x;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,x

(commuting conversion)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}{(\texttt{case}\,\,x^{\prime}\{\texttt{inl}\,\,x.\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,x;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,x\,\,|\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,_{.}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\mho;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,x\})}

(
\underline{F}\beta,\mho
strictness)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}{(\texttt{case}\,\,x^{\prime}\{\texttt{inl}\,\,x.\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,x\,\,|\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,_{.}\mho\})}

(
\mho
bottom)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq{(\texttt{case}\,\,x^{\prime}\{\texttt{inl}\,\,x.\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,x\,\,|\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,y.\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,y\})}

(
+\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime}

∎

Whose proof relies on the following induction principle for the
returner type:

Lemma 4.8 (
\underline{F}(+)
Induction Principle).

\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\cdot:\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})\vdash M_{1}\sqsubseteq M_{2}:\underline{B}

holds if and only if

\Gamma,V_{1}:A_{1}\vdash M_{1}[\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,V_{1}]\sqsubseteq M_{2}[\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,V_{2}]:\underline{B}
and

\Gamma,V_{2}:A_{2}\vdash M_{2}[\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,V_{2}]\sqsubseteq M_{2}[\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,V_{2}]:\underline{B}

This shows why the type tag interpretation works: it makes the dynamic
type in some sense the minimal type with injections from each
G
:
the sum of all value ground types
?\cong\Sigma_{G}G
.

The dynamic computation type
\underline{\text{?`}}
can be naturally defined by a
dual construction, by the following dual argument.
First, we want a computation ep pair from
\underline{G}
to
\underline{\text{?`}}
for each
ground computation type
\underline{G}
.
Specifically, this means we want a stack from
\underline{\text{?`}}
to
\underline{G}
(the
downcast) with an embedding.
The easiest way to get this is if, for each ground computation type

\underline{G}
,
\underline{\text{?`}}
is equivalent to a lazy product of
\underline{G}
and “the
other behaviors”, i.e.,
\underline{\text{?`}}\cong\underline{G}\mathbin{\&}\underline{\text{?`}}_{-\underline{G}}
.
Then the embedding on
\pi
performs the embedded computation, but on

\pi^{\prime}
raises a type error.
The following lemma, dual to lemma
4.7

shows this forms a computation ep pair:

Lemma 4.9 (Lazy Product Projections are Computation Projections).

For any
\underline{B},\underline{B}^{\prime}
, there are computation ep pairs from
\underline{B}

and
\underline{B}^{\prime}
to
\underline{B}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}^{\prime}
where the projections are
\pi

and
\pi^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Define the projection for
\underline{B}
to be
\pi
. Define the embedding
by
z.\{\pi\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,z}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\mho}\}
. Similarly define the projection for

\underline{B}^{\prime}
.
This satisfies retraction:

(
U\beta
)

\displaystyle\pi\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\{\pi\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,z}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\mho}\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\pi\{\pi\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,z}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\mho}\}

(
\mathbin{\&}\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{force}\,\,z

and projection:

\displaystyle\texttt{thunk}\,\,\{\pi\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\pi\texttt{force}\,\,w}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\mho}\}

(
U\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,\{\pi\mapsto{\pi\texttt{force}\,\,w}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\mho}\}

(
\mho
bottom)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,\{\pi\mapsto{\pi\texttt{force}\,\,w}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\pi^{\prime}\texttt{force}\,\,w}\}

(
\mathbin{\&}\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{force}\,\,w

(
U\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}w

∎

From this, we see that the easiest way to construct an interpretation
of the dynamic computation type is to make it a lazy product of all
the ground types
\underline{G}
:
\underline{\text{?`}}\cong\With_{\underline{G}}\underline{G}
.
Using recursive types, we can easily make this a definition of the
interpretations:

Definition 4.10 (Natural Dynamic Type Interpretation).

The following defines a dynamic type interpretation.
We define the types to satisfy the isomorphisms

{?}\cong 1+({?}\times{?})+({?}+{?})+U\underline{\text{?`}}\qquad\underline{\text{?`}}\cong(\underline{\text{?`}}\mathbin{\&}\underline{\text{?`}})\mathbin{\&}({?}\to\underline{\text{?`}})\mathbin{\&}\underline{F}{?}

with the ep pairs defined as in
Lemma
4.7
and

4.9
.

Proof.

We can construct
{?},\underline{\text{?`}}
explicitly using recursive and
corecursive types. Specifically, we make the recursion explicit by
defining open versions of the types:

\displaystyle X,\underline{Y}\vdash{?}_{o}

\displaystyle=1+(X\times X)+(X+X)+U\underline{Y}\,\,\text{val type}

\displaystyle X,\underline{Y}\vdash\underline{\text{?`}}_{o}

\displaystyle=(\underline{Y}\mathbin{\&}\underline{Y})\mathbin{\&}(X\to\underline{Y})\mathbin{\&}\underline{F}X\,\,\text{comp type}

Then we define the types
{?},\underline{\text{?`}}
using a standard encoding:

\displaystyle{?}

\displaystyle=\mu X.{?}_{o}[\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{\text{?`}}_{o}/\underline{Y}]

\displaystyle\underline{\text{?`}}

\displaystyle=\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{\text{?`}}_{o}[\mu X.{?}_{o}/X]

Then clearly by the roll/unroll isomorphism we get the desired
isomorphisms:

\displaystyle{?}

\displaystyle\cong{?}_{o}[\underline{\text{?`}}/\underline{Y},{?}/X]=1+({?}\times{?})+({?}+{?})+U\underline{\text{?`}}

\displaystyle\underline{\text{?`}}

\displaystyle\cong{?}_{c}[{?}/X,\underline{\text{?`}}/\underline{Y}]=(\underline{\text{?`}}\mathbin{\&}\underline{\text{?`}})\mathbin{\&}({?}\to\underline{\text{?`}})\mathbin{\&}\underline{F}{?}

∎

This dynamic type interpretation is a natural fit for CBPV because the
introduction forms for
{?}
are exactly the introduction forms for
all of the value types (unit, pairing,
inl
,
inr
,
force
), while
elimination forms are all of the elimination forms for computation types
(
\pi
,
\pi^{\prime}
, application and binding); such “bityped” languages
are related to
Girard 2001
;
Zeilberger 2009
.

Based on this dynamic type interpretation, we can extend GTT to support
a truly dynamically typed style of programming, where one can perform
case-analysis on the dynamic types at runtime, in addition to the type
assertions provided by upcasts and downcasts.

\frac{\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash V:{?}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma,x_{1}:1\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash E_{1}:T\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma,x_{\times}:{?}\times{?}\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash E_{\times}:T\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma,x_{+}:{?}+{?}\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash E_{+}:T\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma,x_{U}:U\underline{\text{?`}}\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash E_{U}:T\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=${?}$E}\par\texttt{tycase}\,\,(\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{G}\rangle V)\,\{x_{1}.E_{1}\,\,|\,\,x_{\times}.E_{\times}\,\,|\,\,x_{+}.E_{+}\,\,|\,\,x_{U}.E_{U}\}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}E_{G}[V/x_{G}]\hskip 18.49988pt({?}\beta)\par\frac{\Gamma,x:{?}\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash E:\underline{B}}{E\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{tycase}\,\,x\,\{x_{1}.E[\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{1}\rangle/x_{1}]\,\,|\,\,x_{\times}.E[\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{{\times}}\rangle/x_{\times}]\,\,|\,\,x_{+}.E[\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{+}\rangle/x_{+}]\,\,|\,\,x_{U}.E[\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U}\rangle/x_{U}]\}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=${?}\eta$}\par\frac{\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash M_{\to}:{?}\to\underline{\text{?`}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash M_{\mathbin{\&}}:\underline{\text{?`}}\mathbin{\&}\underline{\text{?`}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash M_{\underline{F}}:\underline{F}}{\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash\{\mathbin{\&}\mapsto M_{\mathbin{\&}}\,\,|\,\,(\to)\mapsto M_{\to}\,\,|\,\,\underline{F}\mapsto M_{\underline{F}}\}:\underline{\text{?`}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=$\underline{\text{?`}}$}\par\langle{\underline{G}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle\{\mathbin{\&}\mapsto M_{\mathbin{\&}}\,\,|\,\,(\to)\mapsto M_{\to}\,\,|\,\,\underline{F}\mapsto M_{\underline{F}}\}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}M_{\underline{G}}\hskip 9.24994pt(\underline{\text{?`}}\beta)\par{\bullet:\underline{\text{?`}}\vdash\bullet\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\mathbin{\&}\mapsto\langle{\underline{\text{?`}}\mathbin{\&}\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle\bullet\,\,|\,\,(\to)\mapsto\langle{{?}\to\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle\bullet\,\,|\,\,\underline{F}\mapsto\langle{\underline{F}{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle\bullet\}}\hskip 9.24994pt(\underline{\text{?`}}\eta)

Figure 7.
Natural Dynamic Type Extension of GTT

The axioms we choose might seem to under-specify the dynamic type, but
because of the uniqueness of adjoints, the following are derivable.

Lemma 4.11 (Natural Dynamic Type Extension Theorems).

The following are derivable in GTT with the natural dynamic type extension

{\langle{\underline{F}1}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}{?}}\rangle\texttt{ret}\,\,V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{tycase}\,\,V\,\{x_{1}.\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{1}\,\,|\,\,\texttt{else}\,\,\mho\}}\\
{\langle{\underline{F}({?}\times{?})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}{?}}\rangle\texttt{ret}\,\,V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{tycase}\,\,V\,\{x_{\times}.\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{\times}\,\,|\,\,\texttt{else}\,\,\mho\}}\\
{\langle{\underline{F}({?}+{?})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}{?}}\rangle\texttt{ret}\,\,V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{tycase}\,\,V\,\{x_{+}.\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{+}\,\,|\,\,\texttt{else}\,\,\mho\}}\\
{\langle{\underline{F}U\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}{?}}\rangle\texttt{ret}\,\,V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{tycase}\,\,V\,\{x_{U}.\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{U}\,\,|\,\,\texttt{else}\,\,\mho\}}\\
\texttt{force}\,\,\langle{U\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(\underline{\text{?`}}\mathbin{\&}\underline{\text{?`}})}\rangle V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\mathbin{\&}\mapsto\texttt{force}\,\,V\,\,|\,\,(\to)\mapsto\mho\,\,|\,\,\underline{F}\mapsto\mho\}\\
\texttt{force}\,\,\langle{U\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U({?}\to\underline{\text{?`}})}\rangle V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\mathbin{\&}\mapsto\mho\,\,|\,\,(\to)\mapsto\texttt{force}\,\,V\,\,|\,\,\underline{F}\mapsto\mho\}\\
\texttt{force}\,\,\langle{U\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{F}{?}}\rangle V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\mathbin{\&}\mapsto\mho\,\,|\,\,(\to)\mapsto\mho\,\,|\,\,\underline{F}\mapsto\texttt{force}\,\,V\}\\

We explore this in more detail with the next dynamic type
interpretation.

Next, we easily see that if we want to limit GTT to just the CBV types
(i.e. the only computation types are
A\to\underline{F}A^{\prime}
), then we can
restrict the dynamic types as follows:

Definition 4.12 (CBV Dynamic Type Interpretation).

The following is a dynamic type interpretation for the ground types of
GTT with only function computation types:

{?}\cong 1+({?}+{?})+({?}\times{?})+U(\underline{\text{?`}})\qquad\underline{\text{?`}}\cong{?}\to\underline{F}{?}

And finally if we restrict GTT to only CBN types (i.e., the only value
type is booleans
1+1
), we can restrict the dynamic types as follows:

Definition 4.13 (CBN Dynamic Type Interpretation).

The following is a dynamic type interpretation for the ground types of
GTT with only boolean value types:

{?}=(1+1)\qquad\underline{\text{?`}}\cong(\underline{\text{?`}}\mathbin{\&}\underline{\text{?`}})\mathbin{\&}(U\underline{\text{?`}}\to\underline{\text{?`}})\mathbin{\&}\underline{F}{?}

4.2.2.
Scheme-like Dynamic Type Interpretation

The above dynamic type interpretation does not correspond to any
dynamically typed language used in practice, in part because it
includes explicit cases for the “additives”, the sum type
+
and
lazy product type
\mathbin{\&}
.
Normally, these are not included in this way, but rather sums are
encoded by making each case use a fresh constructor (using nominal
techniques like opaque structs in Racket) and then making the sum the
union of the constructors, as argued in
Siek and
Tobin-Hochstadt 2016
.
We leave modeling this nominal structure to future work, but in
minimalist languages, such as simple dialects of Scheme and Lisp, sum
types are often encoded
structurally
rather than nominally by
using some fixed sum type of
symbols
, also called
atoms
.
Then a value of a sum type is modeled by a pair of a symbol (to indicate
the case) and a payload with the actual value.
We can model this by using the canonical isomorphisms

{?}+{?}\cong((1+1)\times{?})\qquad\underline{\text{?`}}\mathbin{\&}\underline{\text{?`}}\cong(1+1)\to\underline{\text{?`}}

and representing sums as pairs, and lazy products as functions.
The fact that isomorphisms are ep pairs is useful for constructing the
ep pairs needed in the dynamic type interpretation.

Lemma 4.14 (Isomorphisms are EP Pairs).

If
x:A\vdash V^{\prime}:A^{\prime}
and
x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\vdash V:A
are an isomorphism in
that
V[V^{\prime}/x^{\prime}]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}x
and
V[V/x]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}x^{\prime}
, then
(x.V^{\prime},\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;\texttt{ret}\,\,V^{\prime})
are a value ep pair from
A
to

A^{\prime}
. Similarly if
\bullet:\underline{B}\vdash S^{\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}
and
\bullet:\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash S:\underline{B}
are an isomorphism in that
S[S^{\prime}]\equiv\bullet
and
S^{\prime}[S]\equiv\bullet
then
(z.S^{\prime}[\texttt{force}\,\,z],S)
is an
ep pair from
\underline{B}
to
\underline{B}^{\prime}
.

With this in mind, we remove the cases for sums and lazy pairs from the
natural dynamic types, and include some atomic type as a case of

{?}
—for simplicity we will just use booleans.
We also do not need a case for
1
, because we can identify it with one
of the booleans, say
true
.
This leads to the following definition:

Definition 4.15 (Scheme-like Dynamic Type Interpretation).

We can define a dynamic type interpretation with the following type
isomorphisms:

{?}\cong(1+1)+U\underline{\text{?`}}+({?}\times{?})\underline{\text{?`}}\cong({?}\to\underline{\text{?`}})\mathbin{\&}\underline{F}{?}

Proof.

We construct
{?},\underline{\text{?`}}
explicitly as follows.

First define
X:\,\,\text{val type}\vdash\texttt{Tree}[X]\,\,\text{val type}
to be the
type of binary trees:

\texttt{Tree}=\mu X^{\prime}.X+(X^{\prime}\times X^{\prime})

Next, define
X:\,\,\text{val type},\underline{Y}:ctype\vdash\texttt{VarArg}[X,\underline{Y}]\,\,\text{comp type}
to be the type of variable-arity functions from
X
to
\underline{Y}
:

\texttt{VarArg}=\nu\underline{Y}^{\prime}.\underline{Y}\mathbin{\&}(X\to\underline{Y}^{\prime})

Then we define an open version of
{?},\underline{\text{?`}}
with respect to a
variable representing the occurrences of
{?}
in
\underline{\text{?`}}
:

\displaystyle X\,\,\text{val type}\vdash{?}_{o}

\displaystyle=\texttt{Tree}[(1+1)+U\underline{\text{?`}}_{o}]\,\,\text{comp type}

\displaystyle X\,\,\text{val type}\vdash\underline{\text{?`}}_{o}

\displaystyle=\texttt{VarArg}[\underline{F}X/\underline{Y}]\,\,\text{comp type}

Then we can define the closed versions using a recursive type:

{?}=\mu X.{?}_{o}\underline{\text{?`}}=\underline{\text{?`}}_{o}[{?}]

The ep pairs for
\times,U,\underline{F},\to
are clear. To define the
rest, first note that there is an ep pair from
1+1
to
{?}
by
Lemma
4.7
. Next, we can define
1
to
be the ep pair to
1+1
defined by the left case and
Lemma
4.7
, composed with this. The ep
pair for
{?}+{?}
is defined by composing the isomorphism
(which is always an ep pair)

({?}+{?})\cong((1+1)\times{?})
with the ep pair for

1+1
using the action of product types on ep pairs (proven as part of
Theorem
4.23
):
({?}+{?})\cong((1+1)\times{?})\,\triangleleft\,({?}\times{?})\,\triangleleft\,{?}
(where we write
A\triangleleft A^{\prime}
to mean there is an ep
pair from
A
to
A^{\prime}
). Similarly, for
\underline{\text{?`}}\mathbin{\&}\underline{\text{?`}}
, we use
action of the function type on ep pairs (also proven as part of
Theorem
4.23
):
\underline{\text{?`}}\mathbin{\&}\underline{\text{?`}}\cong((1+1)\to\underline{\text{?`}})\,\triangleleft\,({?}\to\underline{\text{?`}})\,\triangleleft\,\underline{\text{?`}}

∎

If we factor out some of the recursion to use inductive and
coinductive types, we get the following isomorphisms:

{?}\cong\texttt{Tree}[(1+1)+U\underline{\text{?`}}]\underline{\text{?`}}\cong\texttt{VarArg}[{?}][\underline{F}{?}]

That is a dynamically typed value is a binary tree whose leaves are
either booleans or closures.
We think of this as a simple type of S-expressions.
A dynamically typed computation is a variable-arity function that is
called with some number of dynamically typed value arguments
{?}

and returns a dynamically typed result
\underline{F}{?}
.
This captures precisely the function type of Scheme, which allows for
variable arity functions!

What’s least clear is
why
the type

\texttt{VarArg}[X][\underline{Y}]=\nu\underline{Y}^{\prime}.(X\to\underline{Y}^{\prime})\mathbin{\&}\underline{Y}

Should be thought of as a type of variable arity functions.
First consider the infinite unrolling of this type:

\texttt{VarArg}[X][\underline{Y}]\simeq\underline{Y}\mathbin{\&}(X\to\underline{Y})\mathbin{\&}(X\to X\to\underline{Y})\mathbin{\&}\cdots

this says that a term of type
\texttt{VarArg}[X][Y]
offers an
infinite number of possible behaviors: it can act as a function from

X^{n}\to\underline{Y}
for any
n
.
Similarly in Scheme, a function can be called with any number of
arguments.
Finally note that this type is isomorphic to a function that takes a

cons-list
of arguments:

\displaystyle\underline{Y}\mathbin{\&}(X\to\underline{Y})\mathbin{\&}(X\to X\to\underline{Y})\mathbin{\&}\cdots

\displaystyle\cong(1\to\underline{Y})\mathbin{\&}((X\times 1)\to\underline{Y})\mathbin{\&}((X\times X\times 1)\to\underline{Y})\mathbin{\&}\cdots

\displaystyle\cong(1+(X\times 1)+(X\times X\times 1)+\cdots)\to\underline{Y}

\displaystyle\cong(\mu X^{\prime}.1+(X\times X^{\prime}))\to\underline{Y}

But operationally the type
\texttt{VarArg}[{?}][\underline{F}{?}]
is a
more faithful model of Scheme implementations because all of the
arguments are passed individually on the stack, whereas the type
(\mu X.1+({?}\times X))\to\underline{F}X
is a function that takes a single
argument that is a list.
These two are distinguished in Scheme and the “dot args” notation
witnesses the isomorphism.

Based on this dynamic type interpretation we can make a “Scheme-like”
extension to GTT in Figure
8
.
First, we add a boolean type
\mathbb{B}
with
true
,
false
and
if-then-else.
Next, we add in the elimination form for
{?}
and the introduction
form for
\underline{\text{?`}}
.
The elimination form for
{?}
is a typed version of Scheme’s

match
macro.
The introduction form for
\underline{\text{?`}}
is a typed, CBPV version of Scheme’s

case-lambda
construct.
Finally, we add type dynamism rules expressing the representations of

1
,
A+A
, and
A\times A
in terms of booleans that were explicit
in the ep pairs used in Definition
4.15
.

1\sqsubseteq\mathbb{B}A+A\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\mathbb{B}\times A\underline{B}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\mathbb{B}\to\underline{B}\par\\
\frac{}{\Gamma\vdash\texttt{true},\texttt{false}:\mathbb{B}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=$\mathbb{B}$I}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash V:\mathbb{B}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma\vdash E_{t}:T\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma\vdash E_{f}:T}{\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash\texttt{if}\,\,V\,\texttt{then}\,\,E_{t}\,\texttt{else}\,\,E_{f}:T}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=$\mathbb{B}$E}\par\\
\texttt{if}\,\,\texttt{true}\,\texttt{then}\,\,E_{t}\,\texttt{else}\,\,E_{f}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}E_{t}\texttt{if}\,\,\texttt{false}\,\texttt{then}\,\,E_{t}\,\texttt{else}\,\,E_{f}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}E_{f}\\
x:\mathbb{B}\vdash E\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{if}\,\,x\,\texttt{then}\,\,E[\texttt{true}/x]\,\texttt{else}\,\,E[\texttt{false}/x]\\
\par\langle{\mathbb{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{1}\rangle V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{true}\langle{\mathbb{B}\times A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A+A}\rangle\texttt{inl}\,\,V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}(\texttt{true},V)\langle{\mathbb{B}\times A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A+A}\rangle\texttt{inr}\,\,V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}(\texttt{false},V)\\
\par\pi\langle{\underline{B}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\mathbb{B}\to\underline{B}}\rangle M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}M\,\texttt{true}\pi^{\prime}\langle{\underline{B}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\mathbb{B}\to\underline{B}}\rangle M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}M\,\texttt{false}\\
\par\frac{\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash M_{\to}:{?}\to\underline{\text{?`}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash M_{\underline{F}}:\underline{F}{?}}{\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash\{(\to)\mapsto M_{\to}\,\,|\,\,\underline{F}\mapsto M_{\underline{F}}\}:\underline{\text{?`}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=$\underline{\text{?`}}$I}\\
\par\langle{\underline{G}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle\{(\to)\mapsto M_{\to}\,\,|\,\,\underline{F}\mapsto M_{\underline{F}}\}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}M_{\underline{G}}\hskip 9.24994pt(\underline{\text{?`}}\beta)\par{\bullet:\underline{\text{?`}}\vdash\bullet\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{(\to)\mapsto\langle{{?}\to\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle\bullet\,\,|\,\,\underline{F}\mapsto\langle{\underline{F}{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle\bullet\}}\hskip 9.24994pt(\underline{\text{?`}}\eta)\\

\par\frac{G\in\{\mathbb{B},\times,U\}}{\texttt{tycase}\,\,(\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{G}\rangle V)\,\{x_{\mathbb{B}}.E_{\mathbb{B}}\,\,|\,\,x_{U}.E_{U}\,\,|\,\,x_{\times}.E_{\times}\}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}E_{G}[V/x_{G}]}\hskip 18.49988pt({?}\beta)\\
\par\frac{\Gamma,x:{?}\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash E:\underline{B}}{E\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{tycase}\,\,x\,\{x_{\mathbb{B}}.E[\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\mathbb{B}}\rangle/x_{\mathbb{B}}]\,\,|\,\,x_{\times}.E[\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{{\times}}\rangle/x_{\times}]\,\,|\,\,x_{U}.E[\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U}\rangle/x_{U}]\}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=${?}\eta$}\\
\par

Figure 8.
Scheme-like Extension to GTT

The reader may be surprised by how
few
axioms we need to add
to GTT for this extension: for instance we only define the upcast
from
1
to
\mathbb{B}
and not vice-versa, and similarly the sum/lazy
pair type isomorphisms only have one cast defined when a priori
there are
4
to be defined.
Finally for the dynamic types we define
\beta
and
\eta
laws
that use the ground casts as injections and projections
respectively, but we don’t define the corresponding dual casts (the
ones that possibly error).

In fact all of these expected axioms can be
proven
from those
we have shown.
Again we see the surprising rigidity of GTT: because an
\underline{F}

downcast is determined by its dual value upcast (and vice-versa for

U
upcasts), we only need to define the upcast as long as the
downcast
could
be implemented already.
Because we give the dynamic types the universal property of a
sum/lazy product type respectively, we can derive the
implementations of the “checking” casts.
All of the proofs are direct from the uniqueness of adjoints
lemma.

Theorem 4.16 (Boolean to Unit Downcast).

In Scheme-like GTT, we can prove

\langle{\underline{F}1}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}\mathbb{B}}\rangle\bullet\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\bullet;\texttt{if}\,\,x\,\texttt{then}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,()\,\texttt{else}\,\,\mho

Theorem 4.17 (Tagged Value to Sum).

In Scheme-like GTT, we can prove

\langle{A+A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\mathbb{B}\times A}\rangle V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{split}\,\,V\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x,y).\texttt{if}\,\,x\,\texttt{then}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,y\,\texttt{else}\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,y

and the downcasts are given by lemma
4.14
.

Theorem 4.18 (Lazy Product to Tag Checking Function).

In Scheme-like GTT, we can prove

\langle{\mathbb{B}\to\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}}\rangle\bullet\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\lambda x:\mathbb{B}.\texttt{if}\,\,x\,\texttt{then}\,\,\pi\bullet\,\texttt{else}\,\,\pi^{\prime}\bullet

and the upcasts are given by lemma
4.14
.

Theorem 4.19 (Ground Mismatches are Errors).

In Scheme-like GTT we can prove

{\langle{\underline{F}\mathbb{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}{?}}\rangle\texttt{ret}\,\,V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{tycase}\,\,V\,\{x_{\mathbb{B}}.\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{\mathbb{B}}\,\,|\,\,\texttt{else}\,\,\mho\}}\\
{\langle{\underline{F}({?}\times{?})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}{?}}\rangle\texttt{ret}\,\,V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{tycase}\,\,V\,\{x_{\times}.\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{\times}\,\,|\,\,\texttt{else}\,\,\mho\}}\\
{\langle{\underline{F}U\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}{?}}\rangle\texttt{ret}\,\,V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{tycase}\,\,V\,\{x_{U}.\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{U}\,\,|\,\,\texttt{else}\,\,\mho\}}\\
\par\texttt{force}\,\,\langle{U\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U({?}\to\underline{\text{?`}})}\rangle V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{(\to)\mapsto\texttt{force}\,\,V\,\,|\,\,\underline{F}\mapsto\mho\}\\
\texttt{force}\,\,\langle{U\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{F}{?}}\rangle V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{(\to)\mapsto\mho\,\,|\,\,\underline{F}\mapsto\texttt{force}\,\,V\}\\

Finally, we note now that all of these axioms are satisfied when
using the Scheme-like dynamic type interpretation and extending the
translation of GTT into CBPV* with the following, tediously
explicit definition:

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathbb{B}\rrbracket=1+1

\displaystyle\llbracket\texttt{true}\rrbracket=\texttt{inl}\,\,()

\displaystyle\llbracket\texttt{false}\rrbracket=\texttt{inr}\,\,()

\displaystyle\llbracket\texttt{if}\,\,V\,\texttt{then}\,\,E_{t}\,\texttt{else}\,\,E_{f}\rrbracket=\texttt{case}\,\,\llbracket V\rrbracket\{x.E_{t}\,\,|\,\,x.E_{f}\}

\displaystyle\llbracket\texttt{tycase}\,\,x\,\{x_{\mathbb{B}}.E_{\mathbb{B}}\,\,|\,\,x_{U}.E_{U}\,\,|\,\,x_{\times}.E_{\times}\}\rrbracket=

\displaystyle\quad\texttt{unroll}\,\,(x:{?})\,\texttt{to}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,x^{\prime}.\texttt{unroll}\,\,x^{\prime}:\texttt{Tree}[(1+1)+U\underline{\text{?`}}]\,\texttt{to}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,t.\texttt{case}\,\,t

\displaystyle\qquad\{{l.\texttt{case}\,\,l\{x_{\mathbb{B}}.\llbracket E_{\mathbb{B}}\rrbracket\,\,|\,\,x_{U}.\llbracket E_{U}\rrbracket\}}

\displaystyle\qquad\,\,|\,\,x_{\times}.\llbracket E_{\times}\rrbracket\}

\displaystyle\llbracket\{(\to)\mapsto M_{\to}\,\,|\,\,\underline{F}\mapsto M_{\underline{F}}\}\rrbracket=\texttt{roll}_{\nu\underline{Y}.({?}\to\underline{Y})\mathbin{\&}\underline{F}{?}}\,\,\{\pi\mapsto{\llbracket M_{\to}\rrbracket}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\llbracket M_{\underline{F}}\rrbracket}\}

4.3.
Contract Translation

Having defined the data parameterizing the translation, we now consider
the translation of GTT into CBPV* itself.
For the remainder of the paper, we assume that we have a fixed dynamic
type interpretation
\rho
, and all proofs and definitions work for any
interpretation.

x:\llbracket A\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket:\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket\bullet:\llbracket\underline{B}^{\prime}\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket:\llbracket\underline{B}\rrbracket\\

Figure 9.
Cast to Contract Translation

4.3.1.
Interpreting Casts as Contracts

The main idea of the translation is an extension of the dynamic type
interpretation to an interpretation of
all
casts in GTT
(Figure
9
) as contracts in CBPV*, following
the definitions in Lemma
3.25
.

Some clauses of the translation are overlapping, which we resolve by
considering them as ordered (though we will ultimately show they are
equivalent).
The definition is also not obviously total: we need to verify that it
covers every possible case where
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
and
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
.
To prove totality and coherence, we could try induction on the type
dynamism relation of Figure
2
, but it is
convenient to first give an alternative, normalized set of rules for
type dynamism that proves the same relations, which we do in
Figure
10
.

\frac{A\in\{{?},1\}}{A\sqsubseteq A}\par\frac{A\in\{{?},0\}}{0\sqsubseteq A}\par\frac{A\sqsubseteq\lfloor A\rfloor A\not\in\{0,{?}\}}{A\sqsubseteq{?}}\\
\par\frac{\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}}{UB\sqsubseteq UB^{\prime}}\par\frac{A_{1}\sqsubseteq A_{1}^{\prime}A_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{2}^{\prime}}{A_{1}+A_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\par\frac{A_{1}\sqsubseteq A_{1}^{\prime}A_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{2}^{\prime}}{A_{1}\times A_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime}}\\
\par\frac{}{\underline{\text{?`}}\sqsubseteq\underline{\text{?`}}}\par\frac{\underline{B}\in\{\underline{\text{?`}},\top\}}{\top\sqsubseteq\underline{B}}\par\frac{\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\lfloor\underline{B}\rfloor\underline{B}\not\in\{\top,\underline{\text{?`}}\}}{\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{\text{?`}}}\\
\par\frac{A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}}{\underline{F}A\sqsubseteq\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\par\frac{\underline{B}_{1}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\underline{B}_{2}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}{\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\par\frac{A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}}{A\to\underline{B}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}

Figure 10.
Normalized Type Dynamism Relation

Lemma 4.20 (Normalized Type Dynamism is Equivalent to Original).

T\sqsubseteq T^{\prime}
is provable in the normalized typed dynamism
definition iff it is provable in the original typed
dynamism definition.

Proof.

It is clear that the normalized system is a subset of the original:
every normalized rule corresponds directly to a rule of the original
system, except the normalized
A\sqsubseteq{?}
and
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{\text{?`}}

rules have a subderivation that was not present originally.

For the converse, first we show by induction that reflexivity is
admissible:

(1)

If
A\in\{{?},1,0\}
, we use a normalized rule.

(2)

If
A\not\in\{{?},1,0\}
, we use the inductive hypothesis
and the monotonicity rule.

(3)

If
\underline{B}\in\{\underline{\text{?`}},\top\}
use the normalized rule.

(4)

If
\underline{B}\not\in\{\underline{\text{?`}},\top\}
use the inductive hypothesis
and monotonicity rule.

Next, we show that transitivity is admissible:

(1)

Assume we have
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}

(a)

If the left rule is
0\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
, then either
A^{\prime}={?}

or
A^{\prime}=0
. If
A^{\prime}=0
the right rule is
0\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}
and we
can use that proof. Otherwise,
A^{\prime}={?}
then the right rule
is
{?}\sqsubseteq{?}
and we can use
0\sqsubseteq{?}
.

(b)

If the left rule is
A\sqsubseteq A
where
A\in\{{?},1\}

then either
A={?}
, in which case
A^{\prime\prime}={?}
and we’re
done. Otherwise the right rule is either
1\sqsubseteq 1
(done) or

1\sqsubseteq{?}
(also done).

(c)

If the left rule is
A\sqsubseteq{?}
with

A\not\in\{0,{?}\}
then the right rule must be
{?}\sqsubseteq{?}
and we’re done.

(d)

Otherwise the left rule is a monotonicity rule for one of

U,+,\times
and the right rule is either monotonicity (use
the inductive hypothesis) or the right rule is
A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq{?}

with a sub-proof of
A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\lfloor A^{\prime}\rfloor
. Since the left rule
is monotonicity,
\lfloor A\rfloor=\lfloor A^{\prime}\rfloor
, so we inductively use
transitivity of the proof of
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
with the proof of
A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\lfloor A^{\prime}\rfloor
to get a proof
A\sqsubseteq\lfloor A\rfloor
and thus

A\sqsubseteq{?}
.

(2)

Assume we have
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}
.

(a)

If the left rule is
\top\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
then
\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}\in\{\underline{\text{?`}},\top\}
so we apply that rule.

(b)

If the left rule is
\underline{\text{?`}}\sqsubseteq\underline{\text{?`}}
, the right rule must
be as well.

(c)

If the left rule is
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{\text{?`}}
the right rule must
be reflexivity.

(d)

If the left rule is a monotonicity rule for
\mathbin{\&},\to,\underline{F}
then the right rule is either also monotonicity (use the
inductive hypothesis) or it’s a
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{\text{?`}}
rule and we
proceed with
{?}
above

Finally we show
A\sqsubseteq{?}
,
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{\text{?`}}
are admissible
by induction on
A
,
\underline{B}
.

(1)

If
A\in\{{?},0\}
we use the primitive rule.

(2)

If
A\not\in\{{?},0\}
we use the
A\sqsubseteq{?}
rule
and we need to show
A\sqsubseteq\lfloor A\rfloor
. If
A=1
, we use the

1\sqsubseteq 1
rule, otherwise we use the inductive hypothesis and
monotonicity.

(3)

If
\underline{B}\in\{\underline{\text{?`}},\top\}
we use the primitive rule.

(4)

If
\underline{B}\not\in\{\underline{\text{?`}},\top\}
we use the
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{\text{?`}}
rule and we need to show
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\lfloor\underline{B}\rfloor
, which
follows by inductive hypothesis and monotonicity.

Every other rule in Figure
2
is a rule of
the normalized system in Figure
10
.∎

Based on normalized type dynamism, we show

Theorem 4.21.

If
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
according to Figure
10
, then there is
a unique complex value
x:A\vdash\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket{x}:A^{\prime}

and
if
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
according to Figure
10
, then there is
a unique complex stack
x:\underline{B}\vdash\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket{x}:\underline{B}^{\prime}

4.3.2.
Interpretation of Terms

Next, we extend the translation of casts to a translation of all terms
by congruence, since all terms in GTT besides casts are
in CBPV*. This satisfies:

Lemma 4.22 (Contract Translation Type Preservation).

If
\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash E:T
in GTT, then
\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\,\,|\,\,\llbracket\Delta\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket E\rrbracket:\llbracket T\rrbracket
in CBPV*.

4.3.3.
Interpretation of Term Dynamism

We have now given an interpretation of the types, terms, and
type dynamism proofs of GTT in CBPV*.
To complete this to form a
model
of GTT, we need to give an
interpretation of the
term dynamism
proofs, which is
established by the
following “axiomatic graduality” theorem.
GTT has
heterogeneous
term dynamism
rules indexed by type dynamism, but CBPV* has only
homogeneous

inequalities between terms, i.e., if
E\sqsubseteq E^{\prime}
, then
E,E^{\prime}
have
the
same
context and types.
Since every type dynamism judgement has an associated contract, we can
translate a heterogeneous term dynamism to a homogeneous inequality

up to contract
. Our next overall goal is to prove

Theorem 4.23 (Axiomatic Graduality).

For any dynamic type interpretation,

\frac{\Phi:\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\Psi:\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\Phi\,\,|\,\,\Psi\vdash M\sqsubseteq M^{\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}}{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\,\,|\,\,\llbracket\Delta^{\prime}\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket M\rrbracket[\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]:\llbracket\underline{B}\rrbracket}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\Phi:\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\Phi\vdash V\sqsubseteq V^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}}{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket V^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]:\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket}

where we define
\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket
to upcast each variable, and

\llbracket\Delta\rrbracket
to downcast
\bullet
if it is nonempty, and if

\Delta=\cdot
, then
M[\llbracket\Delta\rrbracket]=M
.
More explicitly,

(1)

If
\Phi:\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime}
, then there exists
n

such that
\Gamma=x_{1}:A_{1},\ldots,x_{n}:A_{n}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=x_{1}^{\prime}:A_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,x_{n}^{\prime}:A_{n}^{\prime}
where
A_{i}\sqsubseteq A_{i}^{\prime}
for each

i\leq n
.
Then
\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket
is a substitution from
\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
to
\llbracket\Gamma^{\prime}\rrbracket

defined as

\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket=\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket x_{1}/x_{1}^{\prime},\ldots\llbracket\langle{A_{n}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{n}}\rangle\rrbracket x_{n}/x_{n}^{\prime}

(2)

If
\Psi:\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime}
, then we similarly define

\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket
as a “linear substitution”. That is, if
\Delta=\Delta^{\prime}=\cdot
, then
\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket
is an empty substitution and

M[\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket]=M
, otherwise
\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket
is a linear
substitution from
\Delta^{\prime}=\bullet:\underline{B}^{\prime}
to
\Delta=\bullet:\underline{B}
where
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
defined as

\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket=\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket\bullet/\bullet

Relative to previous work on graduality
(
New and Ahmed 2018
)
,
the distinction between complex value upcasts and complex stack
downcasts guides the formulation of the theorem; e.g. using upcasts in
the left-hand theorem would require more thunks/forces.

We now develop some lemmas on the way towards proving this result.
First, to keep proofs high-level, we establish the following cast
reductions that follow easily from
\beta,\eta
principles.

Lemma 4.24 (Cast Reductions).

The following are all provable

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{inl}\,\,V]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{inl}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket[V]

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{inr}\,\,V]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{inr}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[V]

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,V]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow\llbracket\langle{A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,V];\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,x_{1}

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,V]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow\llbracket\langle{A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,V];\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,x_{2}

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}1}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}1}\rangle\rrbracket\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\bullet

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{1}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{1}\rangle\rrbracket[x]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}x

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}\times A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,(V_{1},V_{2})]

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{1}];\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{2}];\texttt{ret}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}\times A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[(V_{1},V_{2})]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}(\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket[V_{1}],\llbracket\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[V_{2}])

\displaystyle(\llbracket\langle{A\to\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket M)\,V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}(\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket M)\,(\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket{V})

\displaystyle(\texttt{force}\,\,(\llbracket\langle{U(A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(A\to\underline{B})}\rangle\rrbracket V))\,V^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle[\texttt{ret}\,\,V^{\prime}];{\texttt{force}\,\,(\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{force}\,\,V\,x))})}

\displaystyle\pi\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket\pi M

\displaystyle\pi^{\prime}\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket\pi^{\prime}M

\displaystyle\pi\texttt{force}\,\,(\llbracket\langle{U(\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2})}\rangle\rrbracket V)\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{force}\,\,\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket{\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\pi\texttt{force}\,\,V)}

\displaystyle\pi^{\prime}\texttt{force}\,\,(\llbracket\langle{U(\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U(\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2})}\rangle\rrbracket V)\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{force}\,\,\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket{\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\pi^{\prime}\texttt{force}\,\,V)}

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}U\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,V]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket\texttt{force}\,\,V

\displaystyle\texttt{force}\,\,\llbracket\langle{U\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{F}A}\rangle\rrbracket[V]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,V;\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle x

Our next goal is to show that from the basic casts being ep pairs, we
can prove that all casts as defined in Figure
9

are ep pairs. Before doing so, we prove the following lemma, which is
used for transitivity (e.g. in the
A\sqsubseteq{?}
rule, which uses a
composition
A\sqsubseteq\lfloor A\rfloor\sqsubseteq{?}
):

Lemma 4.25 (EP Pairs Compose).

(1)

If
(V_{1},S_{1})
is a value ep pair from
A_{1}
to
A_{2}
and

(V_{2},S_{2})
is a value ep pair from
A_{2}
to
A_{3}
, then

(V_{2}[V_{1}],S_{1}[S_{2}])
is a value ep pair from
A_{1}
to
A_{3}
.

(2)

If
(V_{1},S_{1})
is a computation ep pair from
\underline{B}_{1}
to
\underline{B}_{2}
and

(V_{2},S_{2})
is a computation ep pair from
\underline{B}_{2}
to
\underline{B}_{3}
, then

(V_{2}[V_{1}],S_{1}[S_{2}])
is a computation ep pair from
\underline{B}_{1}
to
\underline{B}_{3}
.

Proof.

(1)

First, retraction follows from retraction twice:

S_{1}[S_{2}[\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{2}[V_{1}[x]]]]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}S_{1}[\texttt{ret}\,\,[V_{1}[x]]]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}x

and projection follows from projection twice:

(
\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow S_{1}[S_{2}[\bullet]];\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{2}[V_{1}[x]]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}{\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow S_{1}[S_{2}[\bullet]];\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,[V_{1}[x]];\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{2}[y]}

(Commuting conversion)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow S_{1}[S_{2}[\bullet]];{\texttt{ret}\,\,[V_{1}[x]]});\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{2}[y]

(Projection)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow S_{2}[\bullet];\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{2}[y]

(Projection)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\bullet

(2)

Again retraction follows from retraction twice:

S_{1}[S_{2}[\texttt{force}\,\,V_{2}[V_{1}[z]]]]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}S_{1}[\texttt{force}\,\,V_{1}[z]]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{force}\,\,z

and projection from projection twice:

(
U\beta
)

\displaystyle V_{2}[V_{1}[\texttt{thunk}\,\,S_{1}[S_{2}[\texttt{force}\,\,w]]]]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}V_{2}[V_{1}[\texttt{thunk}\,\,S_{1}[\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,S_{2}[\texttt{force}\,\,w]]]]

(Projection)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq V_{2}[\texttt{thunk}\,\,S_{2}[\texttt{force}\,\,w]]

(Projection)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq w

∎

Lemma 4.26 (Identity EP Pair).

(x.x,\bullet)
is an ep pair (value or computation).

Now, we show that all casts are ep pairs.
The proof is a somewhat tedious, but straightforward calculation.

Lemma 4.27 (Casts are EP Pairs).

(1)

For any
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
, the casts
(x.\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle x\rrbracket,\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket)
are a value ep pair from

\llbracket A\rrbracket
to
\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket

(2)

For any
\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}
, the casts
(z.\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle z\rrbracket,\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket)
are a computation ep
pair from
\llbracket\underline{B}\rrbracket
to
\llbracket\underline{B}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.

Proof.

By induction on normalized type dynamism derivations.

(1)

A\sqsubseteq A
(
A\in\{{?},1\}
), because identity is an ep pair.

(2)

0\sqsubseteq A
(that
A\in\{{?},0\}
is not important):

(a)

Retraction is

x:0\vdash\texttt{ret}\,\,x\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{absurd}\,\,x;\mho:\underline{F}A

which holds by
0\eta

(b)

Projection is

\bullet:\underline{F}A\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow(\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\bullet;\mho);{\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{absurd}\,\,x}\sqsubseteq\bullet:\underline{F}A

Which we calculate:

\displaystyle\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow(\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\bullet;\mho);{\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{absurd}\,\,x}

(comm conv)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\bullet;\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\mho;{\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{absurd}\,\,x}

(Strictness of Stacks)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\bullet;\mho

(
\mho
is
\bot
)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\bullet;\texttt{ret}\,\,y

(
\underline{F}\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\bullet

(3)

+
:

(a)

Retraction is

\displaystyle x:A_{1}+A_{2}\vdash

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[x]]

\displaystyle=\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{case}\,\,x\{x_{1}.\texttt{inl}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket[x_{1}]\,\,|\,\,x_{1}.\texttt{inr}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[x_{2}]\}]

(commuting conversion)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{case}\,\,x

\displaystyle\quad\{{x_{1}.\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket[x_{1}]]}

\displaystyle\quad\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[x_{2}]]\}

(cast computation)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{case}\,\,x

\displaystyle\quad\{{x_{1}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket x_{1}];\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,x_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket x_{2}];\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,x_{2}\}

(IH retraction)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{case}\,\,x\{x_{1}.\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,x_{1}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,x_{2}\}

(
+\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{ret}\,\,x

(b)

For Projection:

\displaystyle\bullet:A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}\vdash

\displaystyle\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}(A_{1}+A_{2})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}(A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime})}\rangle\rrbracket;\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[x]

\displaystyle=\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow(\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}\leftarrow\bullet;\texttt{case}\,\,x^{\prime}\{x_{1}^{\prime}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{1}^{\prime}];\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,x_{1}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}^{\prime}.\cdots\});

\displaystyle\quad\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket

(Commuting Conversion)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\bullet;^{\prime}\texttt{case}\,\,x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\{{x_{1}^{\prime}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{1}^{\prime}];\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket{\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\,\,|\,\,x_{2}^{\prime}.\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,x_{2}^{\prime}];\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket{\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,x_{2}}\}

(Cast Computation)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{thunk}\,\,\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\pi^{\prime}\texttt{force}\,\,w]]}\}

(IH projection)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,\{\pi\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\pi\texttt{force}\,\,w}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\pi^{\prime}\texttt{force}\,\,w}\}

(
U\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,\{\pi\mapsto{\pi\texttt{force}\,\,w}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\pi^{\prime}\texttt{force}\,\,w}\}

(
\mathbin{\&}\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{force}\,\,w

(
U\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}w

(4)

(b)

Projection

\displaystyle w:U(A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime})\vdash

\displaystyle\qquad\texttt{force}\,\,\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{thunk}\,\,((\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A\to\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{force}\,\,w])\,x)]

\displaystyle\qquad\texttt{force}\,\,\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{thunk}\,\,((\llbracket\langle{A\to\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{force}\,\,w])\,x)]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,\lambda x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}.

(cast reduction)

\displaystyle\qquad\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime}];

\displaystyle\qquad\texttt{force}\,\,\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{thunk}\,\,\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[(\texttt{force}\,\,w)\,(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle[x])]]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,\lambda x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}.

(
\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle\qquad\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime}];

\displaystyle\qquad\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,{\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle[x]};

\displaystyle\qquad\texttt{force}\,\,\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{thunk}\,\,\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[(\texttt{force}\,\,w)\,x^{\prime}]]

(IH projection)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,\lambda x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}.

\displaystyle\qquad\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime};

\displaystyle\qquad\texttt{force}\,\,\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{thunk}\,\,\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[(\texttt{force}\,\,w)\,x^{\prime}]]

(
\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,\lambda x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}.\texttt{force}\,\,\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{thunk}\,\,\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[(\texttt{force}\,\,w)\,x^{\prime}]]

(
\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,\lambda x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}.\texttt{force}\,\,\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{thunk}\,\,\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,((\texttt{force}\,\,w)\,x^{\prime})]]

(5)

\underline{F}
:

(a)

To show retraction we need to show

z:U\underline{F}A\vdash\texttt{force}\,\,z\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{ret}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket)]

We calculate:

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{ret}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket)]

(
U\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{ret}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket)]

(comm conv)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{ret}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket]

(IH value retraction)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{ret}\,\,x

(
\underline{F}\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{force}\,\,z
