w:U\underline{F}A^{\prime}\vdash\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,{\texttt{thunk}\,\,\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{force}\,\,w]};\texttt{ret}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket)}\sqsubseteq w:U\underline{B}^{\prime}

We calculate as follows

\displaystyle\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,{\texttt{thunk}\,\,\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{force}\,\,w]};\texttt{ret}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket)}

(
U\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{force}\,\,w]};\texttt{ret}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket)}

(IH value projection)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\texttt{force}\,\,w}

(
U\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}w

∎

While the above was tedious, this pays off greatly in later proofs: this
is the
only
proof in the entire development that needs to inspect
the definition of a “shifted” cast (a downcast between
\underline{F}
types or
an upcast between
U
types).
All later lemmas
have cases for these shifted casts, but
only
use the property
that they are part of an ep pair.
This is one of the biggest advantages of using an explicit syntax for
complex values and complex stacks: the shifted casts are the only ones
that non-trivially use effectful terms, so after this lemma is
established we only have to manipulate values and stacks, which
compose much more nicely than effectful terms.
Conceptually, the main reason we can avoid reasoning about the
definitions of the shifted casts directly is that any two shifted casts
that form an ep pair with the same value embedding/stack projection are
equal:

Lemma 4.28 (Value Embedding determines Projection, Computation Projection determines Embedding).

For any value
x:A\vdash V_{e}:A^{\prime}
and stacks
\bullet:\underline{F}A^{\prime}\vdash S_{1}:\underline{F}A
and
\bullet:\underline{F}A^{\prime}\vdash S_{2}:\underline{F}A
, if

(V_{e},S_{1})
and
(V_{e},S_{2})
are both value ep pairs, then

S_{1}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}S_{2}

Similarly for any values
x:U\underline{B}\vdash V_{1}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}
and
x:U\underline{B}\vdash V_{2}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}
and stack
\bullet:\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash S_{p}:\underline{B}
, if
(V_{1},S_{p})
and
(V_{2},S_{p})
are both computation ep pairs then

V_{1}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}V_{2}

Proof.

By symmetry it is sufficient to show
S_{1}\sqsubseteq S_{2}
.

\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{S_{1}\sqsubseteq S_{1}}{\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow S_{1};\texttt{ret}\,\,x\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\bullet;S_{1}[\texttt{ret}\,\,x]}}{\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow S_{1};\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e}\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\bullet;\texttt{ret}\,\,x}}{\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow S_{1};\texttt{ret}\,\,x\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\bullet;S_{2}[\texttt{ret}\,\,x]}}{\bullet:\underline{F}A^{\prime}\vdash S_{1}\sqsubseteq S_{2}:\underline{F}A}

similarly to show
V_{1}\sqsubseteq V_{2}
:

\frac{\frac{\frac{x:U\underline{B}\vdash\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{force}\,\,V_{2}\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{force}\,\,V_{2}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}}{x:U\underline{B}\vdash\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{force}\,\,x\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,V_{2}]}}{x:U\underline{B}\vdash\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{force}\,\,V_{1}\sqsubseteq\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{force}\,\,V_{2}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}}}{x:U\underline{B}\vdash V_{1}\sqsubseteq V_{2}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}}

∎

The next two lemmas on the way to axiomatic graduality show that
Figure
9
translates
\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle
to the
identity and
\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle}
to the same contract as

\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle
, and similarly for downcasts.
Intuitively, for all connectives except
\underline{F},U
, this is because of
functoriality of the type constructors on values and stacks.
For the
\underline{F},U
cases, we will use the corresponding fact about the
dual cast, i.e., to prove the
\underline{F}A
to
\underline{F}A
downcast is the
identity stack, we know by inductive hypothesis that the
A
to
A

upcast is the identity, and that the identity stack is a projection
for the identity.
Therefore Lemma
4.28
implies that the
\underline{F}A
downcast must be equivalent to the identity.
We now discuss these two lemmas and their proofs in detail.

First, we show that the casts from a type to itself are equivalent to
the identity.
Below, we will use this lemma to prove the reflexivity case of the
axiomatic graduality theorem, and to prove a conservativity result,
which says that a GTT homogeneous term dynamism is the same as a
CBPV* inequality between their translations.

Lemma 4.29 (Identity Expansion).

For any
A
and
\underline{B}
,

x:A\vdash\llbracket\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}x:A\bullet:\underline{B}\vdash\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\bullet:\underline{B}

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
A,\underline{B}
, following the proof that
reflexivity is admissible given in Lemma
4.20
.

(1)

If
A\in\{1,{?}\}
, then
\llbracket\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]=x
.

(2)

If
A=0
, then
\texttt{absurd}\,\,x\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}x
by
0\eta
.

(3)

If
A=U\underline{B}
, then by inductive hypothesis
\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\bullet
. By Lemma
4.26
,

(x.x,\bullet)
is a computation ep pair from
\underline{B}
to
itself. But by Lemma
4.27
,
(\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket[x],\bullet)
is also a computation ep pair so the
result follows by uniqueness of embeddings from computation
projections Lemma
4.28
.

(4)

If
A=A_{1}\times A_{2}
or
A=A_{1}+A_{2}
, the result follows by
the
\eta
principle and inductive hypothesis.

(5)

If
\underline{B}=\underline{\text{?`}}
,
\llbracket\langle{\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle\rrbracket=\bullet
.

(6)

For
\underline{B}=\top
, the result follows by
\top\eta
.

(7)

For
\underline{B}=\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}
or
\underline{B}=A\to\underline{B}^{\prime}
, the
result follows by inductive hypothesis and
\eta
.

(8)

For
\underline{B}=\underline{F}A
, by inductive hypothesis, the downcast is a
projection for the value embedding
x.x
, so the result follows by
identity ep pair and uniqueness of projections from value
embeddings.

∎

Second, we show that a composition of upcasts is translated to the same
thing as a direct upcast, and similarly for downcasts. Below, we will
use this lemma to translate
transitivity
of term dynamism in GTT.

Lemma 4.30 (Cast Decomposition).

For any dynamic type interpretation
\rho
,

\frac{A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}}{x:A\vdash\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket_{\rho}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket_{\rho}[\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket_{\rho}]:A^{\prime\prime}}\par\frac{\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}{\bullet:\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket_{\rho}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket_{\rho}[\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket_{\rho}]}

Proof.

By mutual induction on
A,\underline{B}
.

(1)

A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}

(a)

If
A=0
, we need to show
x:0\vdash\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{0}\rangle\rrbracket[x]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{0}\rangle\rrbracket[x]]:A^{\prime\prime}
which follows by

0\eta
.

(b)

If
A={?}
, then
A^{\prime}=A^{\prime\prime}={?}
, and both casts are
the identity.

(c)

If
A\not\in\{{?},0\}
and
A^{\prime}={?}
, then
A^{\prime\prime}={?}
and
\llbracket\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{{?}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket]=\llbracket\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket
by definition.

(d)

If
A,A^{\prime}\not\in\{{?},0\}
and
A^{\prime\prime}={?}
, then

\lfloor A\rfloor=\lfloor A^{\prime}\rfloor
, which we call
G
and

\llbracket\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket=\llbracket\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{G}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{G}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket]

and

\llbracket\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket]=\llbracket\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{G}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{G}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket]]

so this reduces to the case for
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq G
, below.

(e)

If
A,A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}\not\in\{{?},0\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{case}\,\,x

\displaystyle\qquad\{{x_{1}.\texttt{inl}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket[x_{1}]}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{split}\,\,x\,\texttt{to}\,\,(y,z).(\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket[y],\llbracket\langle{A_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[z])

(definition)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime\prime}\times A_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}\times A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[x]

We need to show

x:U\underline{B}\vdash\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket[x]]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket[x]:U\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}

By composition of ep pairs, we know
(x.\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{U\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{U\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket[x]],\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket])
is a computation ep pair.
Furthermore, by inductive hypothesis, we know

\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket

so then both sides form ep pairs paired with
\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket
, so it follows because computation projections
determine embeddings
4.28
.

.

\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{\underline{\text{?`}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle\rrbracket]=\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{\text{?`}}}\rangle\rrbracket

so the result follows from the case

(e)

If
\underline{B},\underline{B}^{\prime},\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}\not\in\{\underline{\text{?`}},\top\}
, then they all have the
same top-level constructor:

(i)

\mathbin{\&}

and

and we need to show

\bullet:\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket]:\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}

We proceed as follows:

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket]

(
\mathbin{\&}\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\pi\mapsto{\pi\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket]}

\displaystyle\quad\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\pi^{\prime}\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket]}\}

(cast reduction)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\pi\mapsto{\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\pi\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket]}

\displaystyle\quad\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\pi^{\prime}\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket]}\}

(cast reduction)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\pi\mapsto{\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\pi\bullet]]}

\displaystyle\quad\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\pi^{\prime}\bullet]}\}

(IH)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\pi\mapsto{\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\pi\bullet]}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\pi^{\prime}\bullet]}\}

(definition)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket

(ii)

\to
, assume we are given
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}
and

\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}
, then we proceed:

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{A\to\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket]

(
\to\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\lambda x:A.(\llbracket\langle{A\to\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket][\bullet])\,x

(cast reduction)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\lambda x:A.\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[(\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime\prime}\to\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\bullet])\,\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]]

(cast reduction)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\lambda x:A.\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\bullet\,\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]]]]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\lambda x:A.\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\bullet\,\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]]

(definition)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\langle{A\to\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A\to\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\bullet]

(iii)

\underline{F}A\sqsubseteq\underline{F}A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{F}A^{\prime\prime}
. First, by
composition of ep pairs, we know

(x.\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]],\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket)[\llbracket\langle{\underline{F}A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{F}A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket]

form a value ep pair.
Furthermore, by inductive hypothesis, we know

x:A\vdash\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]

so the two sides of our equation are both projections with the
same value embedding, so the equation follows from uniqueness
of projections from value embeddings.

∎

The final lemma before the graduality theorem lets us “move a cast”
from left to right or vice-versa, via the adjunction property for ep
pairs.
These arise in the proof cases for
return
and
thunk
, because in those
cases the inductive hypothesis is in terms of an upcast (downcast) and
the conclusion is in terms of a a downcast (upcast).

Lemma 4.31 (Hom-set formulation of Adjunction).

For any value embedding-projection pair
V_{e},S_{p}
from
A
to
A^{\prime}
,
the following are equivalent:

\frac{\Gamma\vdash\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e}[V]\sqsubseteq M:\underline{F}A^{\prime}}{\Gamma\vdash\texttt{ret}\,\,V\sqsubseteq S_{p}[M]:\underline{F}A}

For any computation ep pair
(V_{e},S_{p})
from
\underline{B}
to
\underline{B}^{\prime}
, the
following are equivalent:

\frac{\Gamma,z^{\prime}:U\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash M\sqsubseteq S[S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,z^{\prime}]]:\underline{C}}{\Gamma,z:U\underline{B}\vdash M[V_{e}/z^{\prime}]\sqsubseteq S[\texttt{force}\,\,z]:\underline{C}}

Proof.

(1)

Assume
\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e}[V]\sqsubseteq M:\underline{F}A^{\prime}
. Then by retraction,

\texttt{ret}\,\,V\sqsubseteq S_{p}[\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e}[V]]
so by transitivity, the result
follows by substitution:

\frac{S_{p}\sqsubseteq S_{p}\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e}[V]\sqsubseteq M}{S_{p}[\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e}[V]]\sqsubseteq M}

(2)

Assume
\texttt{ret}\,\,V\sqsubseteq S_{p}[M]:\underline{F}A
. Then by projection,

\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow S_{p}[M];\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e}[x]\sqsubseteq M
, so it is sufficient to show

\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e}[V]\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow S_{p}[M];\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e}[x]

but again by substitution we have

\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,V;\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e}[x]\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow S_{p}[M];\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e}[x]

and by
\underline{F}\beta
, the LHS is equivalent to
\texttt{ret}\,\,V_{e}[V]
.

(3)

Assume
z^{\prime}:U\underline{B^{\prime}}\vdash M\sqsubseteq S[S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,z^{\prime}]]
, then
by projection,
S[S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,V_{e}]]\sqsubseteq S[\texttt{force}\,\,z]

and by substitution:

\frac{M\sqsubseteq S[S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,z^{\prime}]]V_{e}\sqsubseteq V_{e}S[S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,V_{e}]]=(S[S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,z^{\prime}]])[V_{e}/z^{\prime}]}{M[V_{e}/z^{\prime}]\sqsubseteq S[S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,V_{e}]]}

(4)

Assume
z:U\underline{B}\vdash M[V_{e}/z^{\prime}]\sqsubseteq S[\texttt{force}\,\,z]
. Then
by retraction,
M\sqsubseteq M[V_{e}[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,z]}]]
and by
substitution:

M[V_{e}[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,z]}]]\sqsubseteq S[\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,{S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,z]}]

and the right is equivalent to
S[S_{p}[\texttt{force}\,\,z]]
by
U\beta
.

∎

Finally, we prove the axiomatic graduality theorem.
In addition to the lemmas above, the main task is to prove the
“compatibility” cases which are the congruence cases for introduction
and elimination rules.
These come down to proving that the casts “commute” with
introduction/elimination forms, and are all simple calculations.

Theorem 0 (Axiomatic Graduality).

For any dynamic type interpretation, the following are true:

\frac{\Phi:\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\Psi:\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\Phi\,\,|\,\,\Psi\vdash M\sqsubseteq M^{\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}}{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\,\,|\,\,\llbracket\Delta^{\prime}\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket M\rrbracket[\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]:\llbracket\underline{B}\rrbracket}\par\frac{\Phi:\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\Phi\vdash V\sqsubseteq V^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}}{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket V^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]:\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket}

Proof.

By mutual induction over term dynamism derivations. For the
\beta,\eta
and reflexivity rules, we use the identity expansion lemma and
the corresponding
\beta,\eta
rule of
CBPV*
4.29
.

For compatibility rules a pattern emerges. Universal rules
(positive intro, negative elim) are easy, we don’t need to reason about
casts at all. For “(co)-pattern matching rules” (positive elim,
negative intro), we need to invoke the
\eta
principle (or
commuting conversion, which is derived from the
\eta
principle).
In all compatibility cases, the cast reduction lemma keeps the
proof straightforward.

Fortunately, all reasoning about “shifted” casts is handled in
lemmas, and here we only deal with the “nice” value upcasts/stack
downcasts.

(1)

Transitivity for values: The GTT rule is

\frac{\Phi:\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime}\Phi^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\Phi^{\prime\prime}:\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\quad\Phi\vdash V\sqsubseteq V^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\quad\Phi^{\prime}\vdash V^{\prime}\sqsubseteq V^{\prime\prime}:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}\quad}{\Phi^{\prime\prime}\vdash V\sqsubseteq V^{\prime\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}}

Which under translation (and the same assumptions about the contexts) is

\frac{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket V^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]:\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket\quad\llbracket\Gamma^{\prime}\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V^{\prime}\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket V^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi^{\prime}\rrbracket]:\llbracket A^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket}{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket V^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket]:\llbracket A^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket}

We proceed as follows, the key lemma here is the cast decomposition lemma:

(cast decomposition)

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V\rrbracket]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V\rrbracket]]

(IH)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]

(IH)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\llbracket V^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi^{\prime}\rrbracket][\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]

(cast decomposition)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket V^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket]

(2)

Transitivity for terms:
The GTT rule is

\frac{\Phi:\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime}\Phi^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\Phi^{\prime\prime}:\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\Psi:\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime}\Psi:\Delta^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Psi^{\prime\prime}:\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime\prime}\quad\Phi\,\,|\,\,\Psi\vdash M\sqsubseteq M^{\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\quad\Phi^{\prime}\,\,|\,\,\Psi^{\prime}\vdash M^{\prime}\sqsubseteq M^{\prime\prime}:\underline{B}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}\quad}{\Phi^{\prime\prime}\,\,|\,\,\Psi^{\prime\prime}\vdash M\sqsubseteq M^{\prime\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}

Which under translation (and the same assumptions about the contexts) is

\frac{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\,\,|\,\,\llbracket\Delta^{\prime}\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket M\rrbracket[\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]:\llbracket\underline{B}\rrbracket\quad\llbracket\Gamma^{\prime}\rrbracket\,\,|\,\,\llbracket\Delta^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Psi^{\prime}\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi^{\prime}\rrbracket]]:\llbracket\underline{B}^{\prime}\rrbracket}{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\,\,|\,\,\llbracket\Delta^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket M\rrbracket[\llbracket\Psi^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket]]:\llbracket\underline{B}\rrbracket}

We proceed as follows, the key lemma here is the cast decomposition lemma:

(Cast decomposition)

\displaystyle\llbracket M\rrbracket[\llbracket\Psi^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket M\rrbracket[\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket][\llbracket\Psi^{\prime}\rrbracket]

(IH)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Psi^{\prime}\rrbracket][\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]

(IH)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi^{\prime}\rrbracket][\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]]

(Cast decomposition)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket]]

(3)

Substitution of a value in a value:
The GTT rule is

\frac{\Phi,x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:A_{1}\sqsubseteq A_{1}^{\prime}\vdash V_{2}\sqsubseteq V_{2}^{\prime}:A_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{2}^{\prime}\quad\Phi\vdash V_{1}\sqsubseteq V_{1}^{\prime}:A_{1}\sqsubseteq A_{1}^{\prime}}{\Phi\vdash V_{2}[V_{1}/x]\sqsubseteq V_{2}^{\prime}[V_{1}^{\prime}/x^{\prime}]:A_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{2}^{\prime}}

Where
\Phi:\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime}
. Under translation, we need to show

\frac{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,x:\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V_{2}\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket V_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket][\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket[x]/x^{\prime}]:\llbracket A_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket\quad\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V_{1}\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket V_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]:\llbracket A_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket}{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V_{2}[V_{1}/x]\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket V_{2}^{\prime}[V_{1}^{\prime}/x^{\prime}]\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]:\llbracket A_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket}

Which follows by compositionality:

(Compositionality)

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V_{2}[V_{1}/x]\rrbracket]

\displaystyle=(\llbracket\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V_{2}\rrbracket])[\llbracket V_{1}\rrbracket/x]

(IH)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\llbracket V_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket][\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket[x]/x^{\prime}][\llbracket V_{1}\rrbracket/x]

\displaystyle=\llbracket V_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket][\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V_{1}\rrbracket]/x^{\prime}]

(IH)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\llbracket V_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket][\llbracket V_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]/x^{\prime}]

\displaystyle=\llbracket V_{2}^{\prime}[V_{1}^{\prime}/x^{\prime}]\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]

(4)

Substitution of a value in a term:
The GTT rule is

\frac{\Phi,x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\,\,|\,\,\Psi\vdash M\sqsubseteq M^{\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\quad\Phi\vdash V\sqsubseteq V^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}}{\Phi\vdash M[V/x]\sqsubseteq M^{\prime}[V^{\prime}/x^{\prime}]:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}}

Where
\Phi:\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Psi:\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime}
.
Under translation this is:

\frac{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,x:\llbracket A\rrbracket\,\,|\,\,\llbracket\Delta\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket M\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket][\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]/x^{\prime}]]:\llbracket\underline{B}\rrbracket\quad\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[{\llbracket V\rrbracket}]\sqsubseteq\llbracket V^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]:\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket}{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\,\,|\,\,\llbracket\Delta\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket M[V/x]\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime}[V^{\prime}/x^{\prime}]\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]:\llbracket\underline{B}\rrbracket}

Which follows from compositionality of the translation:

(Compositionality)

\displaystyle\llbracket M[V/x]\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket M\rrbracket[\llbracket V\rrbracket/x]

(IH)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket][\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]/x^{\prime}]][\llbracket V\rrbracket/x]

\displaystyle=\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket][\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V\rrbracket]/x^{\prime}]]

(IH)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket][\llbracket V^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]/x^{\prime}]]

(Compositionality)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime}[V^{\prime}/x^{\prime}]\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]

(5)

Substitution of a term in a stack:
The GTT rule is

\frac{\Phi\,\,|\,\,\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash S\sqsubseteq S^{\prime}:\underline{C}\sqsubseteq\underline{C}^{\prime}\quad\Phi\,\,|\,\,\cdot\vdash M\sqsubseteq M^{\prime}:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}}{\Phi\,\,|\,\,\cdot\vdash S[M]\sqsubseteq S^{\prime}[M^{\prime}]:\underline{C}\sqsubseteq\underline{C}^{\prime}}

Where
\Phi:\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime}
.
Under translation this is

\frac{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\,\,|\,\,\bullet:\llbracket\underline{B}^{\prime}\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket S\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\bullet]]\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{C}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket S^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]:\llbracket\underline{C}\rrbracket\quad\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\,\,|\,\,\cdot\vdash\llbracket M\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]:\llbracket\underline{B}\rrbracket}{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\,\,|\,\,\cdot\vdash\llbracket S[M]\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{C}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket S^{\prime}[M^{\prime}]\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]:\llbracket\underline{C}\rrbracket}

We follows easily using compositionality of the translation:

(Compositionality)

\displaystyle\llbracket S[M]\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket S\rrbracket[\llbracket M\rrbracket]

(IH)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\llbracket S\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]]

(IH)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{C}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket S^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket][\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]]

(Compositionality)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\langle{\underline{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{C}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket S^{\prime}[M^{\prime}]\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]

(6)

Variables: The GTT rule is

\Gamma_{1}\sqsubseteq\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}\sqsubseteq\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\vdash x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}

which under translation is

\llbracket\Gamma_{1}\rrbracket,x:\llbracket A\rrbracket,\llbracket\Gamma_{2}\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]:\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket

which is an instance of reflexivity.

(7)

Hole: The GTT rule is

\Phi\,\,|\,\,\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}

which under translation is

\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\,\,|\,\,\bullet:\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\bullet]\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\bullet]:\underline{B}

which is an instance of reflexivity.

(8)

Error is bottom: The GTT axiom is

\Phi\vdash\mho\sqsubseteq M:\underline{B}

where
\Phi:\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime}
, so we need to show

\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\mho\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]]:\llbracket\underline{B}\rrbracket

which is an instance of the error is bottom axiom of CBPV.

(9)

Error strictness: The GTT axiom is

\Phi\vdash S[\mho]\sqsubseteq\mho:\underline{B}

where
\Phi:\Gamma\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime}
, which under translation is

\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket S\rrbracket[\mho]\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket[\mho]:\llbracket\underline{B}\rrbracket

By strictness of stacks in CBPV, both sides are equivalent to

\mho
, so it follows by reflexivity.

(10)

UpCast-L: The GTT axiom is

x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\vdash\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}

which under translation is

x:\llbracket A\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]]\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]:A^{\prime}

Which follows by identity expansion and reflexivity.

(11)

UpCast-R: The GTT axiom is

x:A\vdash x\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle x:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}

which under translation is

x:\llbracket A\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x]]:\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket

which follows by identity expansion and reflexivity.

(12)

DnCast-R: The GTT axiom is

\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\bullet\sqsubseteq\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle:\underline{B}

Which under translation is

\bullet:\llbracket\underline{B}^{\prime}\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\bullet]\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\bullet]]:\llbracket\underline{B}\rrbracket

Which follows by identity expansion and reflexivity.

(13)

DnCast-L: The GTT axiom is

\bullet:\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\bullet\sqsubseteq\bullet:\underline{B}\sqsubseteq\underline{B}^{\prime}

So under translation we need to show

\bullet:\llbracket\underline{B}^{\prime}\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\bullet]]\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket\bullet:\llbracket\underline{B}\rrbracket

Which follows immediately by reflexivity and the lemma that
identity casts are identities.

(14)

0
elim, we do the term case, the value case is similar

\frac{\langle{0}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{0}\rangle[\llbracket V\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket V^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]}{\texttt{absurd}\,\,\llbracket V\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\texttt{absurd}\,\,\llbracket V^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]}

Immediate by
0\eta
.

(15)

+
intro, we do the
inl
case, the
inr
case is the same:

\frac{\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket V^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]}{\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{inl}\,\,\llbracket V\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\texttt{inl}\,\,\llbracket V^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]}

Which follows easily:

(cast reduction)

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{inl}\,\,\llbracket V\rrbracket]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{inl}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket\llbracket V\rrbracket

(IH)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\texttt{inl}\,\,\llbracket V^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]

(16)

+
elim, we do just the cases where the continuations are terms:

\frac{\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket V\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket V^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]\quad\llbracket M_{1}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket M_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket][\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket[x_{1}]/x_{1}^{\prime}]\quad\llbracket M_{2}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket]\sqsubseteq\llbracket M_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket][\llbracket\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[x_{2}]/x_{2}^{\prime}]}{\texttt{case}\,\,\llbracket V\rrbracket\{x_{1}.\llbracket M_{1}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket]\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\llbracket M_{2}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket]\}\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{case}\,\,\llbracket V\rrbracket^{\prime}[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]\{x_{1}^{\prime}.\llbracket M_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]\,\,|\,\,x_{2}^{\prime}.\llbracket M_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket]\}]}

\displaystyle\texttt{case}\,\,\llbracket V\rrbracket\{x_{1}.\llbracket M_{1}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket]\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\llbracket M_{2}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Psi\rrbracket]\}

(IH)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{case}\,\,\llbracket V\rrbracket\{x_{1}.\llbracket M_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket][\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket[x_{1}]/x_{1}^{\prime}]\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\llbracket M_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket][\llbracket\langle{A_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{2}}\rangle\rrbracket[x_{2}]/x_{2}^{\prime}]\}]

(comm conv)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{case}\,\,\llbracket V\rrbracket

\displaystyle\qquad\{{x_{1}.\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket M_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket[\llbracket\Phi\rrbracket][\llbracket\langle{A_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{1}}\rangle\rrbracket[x_{1}]/x_{1}^{\prime}]]}

(
\underline{F}\beta

\displaystyle\qquad\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,\llbracket\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\rrbracket[x];\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket N\rrbracket[\Phi]]

(Projection)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}\leftarrow\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\Phi];\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\llbracket N\rrbracket[\Phi]]

(commuting conversion)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\llbracket\langle{\underline{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\underline{B}^{\prime}}\rangle\rrbracket[\texttt{bind}\,\,x^{\prime}\leftarrow\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\Phi];\llbracket N\rrbracket[\Phi]]

∎

As a corollary, we have the following conservativity result, which says
that the homogeneous term dynamisms in GTT are sound and complete for
inequalities in CBPV*.

Corollary 4.32 (Conservativity).

If
\Gamma\mid\Delta\vdash E,E^{\prime}:T
are two terms of the same
type in the intersection of GTT and CBPV*, then
\Gamma\mid\Delta\vdash E\sqsubseteq E^{\prime}:T
is provable in GTT iff it is
provable in CBPV*.

Proof.

The reverse direction holds because CBPV* is a syntactic subset of
GTT. The forward direction holds by axiomatic graduality and the
fact that identity casts are identities.
∎

5.
Complex Value/Stack Elimination

Next, to bridge the gap between the semantic notion of complex value
and stack with the more rigid operational notion, we perform a
complexity-elimination pass.
This translates a computation with complex values in it to an equivalent
computation without complex values: i.e., all pattern matches take place
in computations, rather than in values, and translates a term dynamism
derivation that uses complex stacks to one that uses only “simple”
stacks without pattern-matching and computation introduction forms.
Stacks do not appear anywhere in the grammar of terms, but they are
used in the equational theory (computation
\eta
rules and error
strictness).
This translation clarifies the behavioral meaning of complex values and
stacks, following
Munch-Maccagnoni 2014
;
Führmann 1999
, and therefore of upcasts and downcasts.
This is related to completeness of focusing: it moves inversion rules
outside of focus phases.

The syntax of operational CBPV is as in
Figure
1
(unshaded), but with recursive
types added as in Section
4.1
, and with values and stacks
restricted
as in Figure
11
.

\begin{array}[]{lcl}A&\mathrel{\bf::=}&X\mid\mu X.A\mid U\underline{B}\mid 0\mid A_{1}+A_{2}\mid 1\mid A_{1}\times A_{2}\\
\underline{B}&::=&\underline{Y}\mid\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}\mid\underline{F}A\mid\top\mid\underline{B}_{1}\mathbin{\&}\underline{B}_{2}\mid A\to\underline{B}\\
\Gamma&::=&\cdot\mid\Gamma,x:A\\
\Delta&::=&\cdot\mid\bullet:\underline{B}\\
V&::=&x\mid\texttt{roll}_{\mu X.A}\,\,V\mid\texttt{inl}\,\,{V}\mid\texttt{inr}\,\,{V}\mid()\mid(V_{1},V_{2})\mid\texttt{thunk}\,\,{M}\\
M&::=&\mho_{\underline{B}}\mid\texttt{let}\,\,x=V;M\mid\texttt{unroll}\,\,V\,\texttt{to}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,x.M\mid\texttt{roll}_{\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}}\,\,M\mid\texttt{unroll}\,\,M\mid\texttt{abort}\,\,{V}\mid\\
&&\texttt{case}\,\,V\{x_{1}.M_{1}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.M_{2}\}\mid\texttt{split}\,\,V\,\texttt{to}\,\,().M\mid\texttt{split}\,\,V\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x,y).M\mid\texttt{force}\,\,{V}\mid\\
&&\texttt{ret}\,\,{V}\mid\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;{N}\mid\lambda x:A.M\mid M\,V\mid\{\}\mid\{\pi\mapsto{M_{1}}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{M_{2}}\}\mid\pi M\mid\pi^{\prime}M\\
S&::=&\bullet\mid\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow S;M\mid S\,V\mid\pi S\mid\pi^{\prime}S\mid\texttt{unroll}_{\nu\underline{Y}.\underline{B}}\,\,{S}\end{array}

Operational CBPV Syntax

In CBPV, values include only introduction forms, as usual for values in
operational semantics, and CBPV stacks consist only of elimination
forms for computation types
(the syntax of CBPV enforces an A-normal
form, where only values can be pattern-matched on, so
case
and

split
are not evaluation contexts in the operational semantics).

CBPV logical and error rules

Levy 2003
translates CBPV* to CBPV, but not does not prove
the inequality preservation that we require here, so we give
an
alternative translation for which this property is easy to
verify .
We translate both complex values and complex
stacks to fully general computations, so that computation
pattern-matching can replace the pattern-matching in complex values/stacks.
For example, for a closed value, we could “evaluate away”
the complexity and get a closed simple value (if we don’t use
U
), but
for open terms, evaluation will get “stuck” if we pattern match on
a variable—so not every complex value can be translated to a value in
CBPV.
More formally, we translate a CBPV* complex value
V:A
to a
CBPV computation
{V}^{{\dagger}}:\underline{F}A
that in CBPV* is equivalent
to
\texttt{ret}\,\,V
.
Similarly, we translate a CBPV* complex stack
S
with hole

\bullet:\underline{B}
to a CBPV computation
{S}^{{\dagger}}
with a free
variable
z:U\underline{B}
such that in CBPV*,
{S}^{{\dagger}}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}S[\texttt{force}\,\,z]
.
Computations
M:\underline{B}
are translated to computations
{M}^{{\dagger}}
with
the same type.

The
de-complexification
procedure is defined as follows.
We note that this translation is not the one presented in

Levy 2003
, but rather a more inefficient version that, in CPS
terminology, introduces many administrative redices.
Since we are only proving results up to observational equivalence
anyway, the difference doesn’t change any of our theorems, and makes
some of the proofs simpler.

Definition 5.1 (De-complexification).

We define

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\bullet}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{force}\,\,z\\
{x}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{ret}\,\,x\\
\\
{({\texttt{ret}\,\,V})}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{V}^{{\dagger}};\texttt{ret}\,\,x\\
{({M\,V})}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{V}^{{\dagger}};{M}^{{\dagger}}\,x\\
\\
{({\texttt{force}\,\,V})}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{V}^{{\dagger}};\texttt{force}\,\,x\\
{({\texttt{absurd}\,\,V})}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{V}^{{\dagger}};\texttt{absurd}\,\,x\\
{({\texttt{case}\,\,V\{x_{1}.E_{1}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.E_{2}\}})}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{V}^{{\dagger}};\texttt{case}\,\,x\{x_{1}.{E_{1}}^{{\dagger}}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.{E_{2}}^{{\dagger}}\}\\
{({\texttt{split}\,\,V\,\texttt{to}\,\,().E})}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{bind}\,\,w\leftarrow V;{\texttt{split}\,\,w\,\texttt{to}\,\,().{E}^{{\dagger}}}\\
{({\texttt{split}\,\,V\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x,y).E})}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{bind}\,\,w\leftarrow V;{\texttt{split}\,\,w\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x,y).{E}^{{\dagger}}}\\
{({\texttt{unroll}\,\,V\,\texttt{to}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,x.E})}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow{V}^{{\dagger}};\texttt{unroll}\,\,y\,\texttt{to}\,\,\texttt{roll}\,\,x.{E}^{{\dagger}}\\
\\
{({\texttt{inl}\,\,V})}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{V}^{{\dagger}};\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,x\\
{({\texttt{inr}\,\,V})}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{V}^{{\dagger}};\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,x\\
{()}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{ret}\,\,()\\
{(V_{1},V_{2})}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{1}\leftarrow{V_{1}}^{{\dagger}};\texttt{bind}\,\,x_{2}\leftarrow{V_{2}}^{{\dagger}};\texttt{ret}\,\,(x_{1},x_{2})\\
{({\texttt{thunk}\,\,M})}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,{M}^{{\dagger}}\\
{({\texttt{roll}\,\,V})}^{{\dagger}}&=&\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{V}^{{\dagger}};\texttt{roll}\,\,x\\
\end{array}

The translation is type-preserving and the identity from CBPV*’s point of view

Lemma 5.2 (De-complexification De-complexifies).

For any CBPV* term
\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash E:T
,
{E}^{{\dagger}}

is a term of CBPV satisfying
\Gamma,{\Delta}^{{\dagger}}\vdash{E}^{{\dagger}}:{T}^{{\dagger}}
where

{\cdot}^{{\dagger}}=\cdot

{({\bullet:\underline{B}})}^{{\dagger}}=z:U\underline{B}
,

{\underline{B}}^{{\dagger}}=\underline{B}
,
{A}^{{\dagger}}=\underline{F}A
.

Lemma 5.3 (De-complexification is Identity in CBPV*).

Considering CBPV as a subset of CBPV*  we have

(1)

If
\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\cdot\vdash M:\underline{B}
then
M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}{M}^{{\dagger}}
.

(2)

If
\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash S:\underline{B}
then
S[\texttt{force}\,\,z]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}{S}^{{\dagger}}
.

(3)

If
\Gamma\vdash V:A
then
\texttt{ret}\,\,V\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}{V}^{{\dagger}}
.

Furthermore, if
M,V,S
are in CBPV, the proof holds in CBPV.

Finally, we need to show that the translation preserves inequalities
(
{E}^{{\dagger}}\sqsubseteq{E^{\prime}}^{{\dagger}}
if
E\sqsubseteq E^{\prime}
), but because complex
values and stacks satisfy more equations than arbitrary computations in
the types of their translations do, we need to isolate the special
“purity” property that their translations have.
We show that complex values are translated to computations that satisfy

thunkability

(
Munch-Maccagnoni 2014
)
, which
intuitively means
M
should have no observable effects, and so
can be freely duplicated or discarded like a value.
In the inequational theory of CBPV, this is defined by saying that
running
M
to a value and then duplicating its value is the same as
running
M
every time we need its value:

Definition 5.4 (Thunkable Computation).

A computation
\Gamma\vdash M:\underline{F}A
is
thunkable
if

\Gamma\vdash\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,M)}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;\texttt{ret}\,\,{(\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{ret}\,\,x))}:\underline{F}U\underline{F}A

Dually, we show that complex stacks are translated to computations that
satisfy (semantic)
linearity

(
Munch-Maccagnoni 2014
)
, where intuitively a computation
M

with a free variable
x:U\underline{B}
is linear in
x
if
M
behaves as if
when it is forced, the first thing it does is forces
x
, and that is the only time
it uses
x
. This is described in the CBPV inequational theory as
follows:
if we have a thunk
z:U\underline{F}U\underline{B}
, then either we can force
it now and pass the result to
M
as
x
, or we can just run
M
with a
thunk that will force
z
each time
M
is forced—but if
M
forces

x
exactly once, first, these two are the same.

Definition 5.5 (Linear Term).

A term
\Gamma,x:U\underline{B}\vdash M:\underline{C}
is
linear in
x

if

\Gamma,z:U\underline{F}U\underline{B}\vdash\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}M[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow(\texttt{force}\,\,z);\texttt{force}\,\,x)}]

Thunkability/linearity of the translations of complex values/stacks are
used to prove the preservation of the
\eta
principles for positive
types and the strictness of complex stacks with respect to errors under
decomplexification.

We need a few lemmas about thunkables and linears to prove that complex
values become thunkable and complex stacks become linear.

First, the following lemma is useful for optimizing programs with
thunkable subterms. Intuitively, since a thunkable has “no effects”
it can be reordered past any other effectful binding. Furhmann

(
Führmann 1999
)
calls a morphism that has this property

central
(after the center of a group, which is those elements
that commute with every element of the whole group).

Lemma 5.6 (Thunkable are Central).

If
\Gamma\vdash M:\underline{F}A
is thunkable and
\Gamma\vdash N:\underline{F}A^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,x:A,y:A^{\prime}\vdash N^{\prime}:\underline{B}
, then

\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;N^{\prime}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;N^{\prime}

Proof.

\displaystyle\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;N^{\prime}

(
U\beta,\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,x;N^{\prime}

(
\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;\texttt{bind}\,\,w\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,x;\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,w;N^{\prime}

(
\underline{F}\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,w\leftarrow(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;{\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,x});\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,w;N^{\prime}

(
M
thunkable)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,w\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M;\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,w;N^{\prime}

(
\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M;N^{\prime}

(
U\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;N^{\prime}

∎

Next, we show thunkables are closed under composition and that return
of a value is always thunkable. This allows us to easily build up
bigger thunkables from smaller ones.

Lemma 5.7 (Thunkables compose).

If
\Gamma\vdash M:\underline{F}A
and
\Gamma,x:A\vdash N:\underline{F}A^{\prime}

are thunkable, then

\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;N

is thunkable.

Proof.

\displaystyle\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;N);\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,y

(
\underline{F}\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,y

(
N
thunkable)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,N

(
\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,x;N)

(
\underline{F}\beta,U\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;\texttt{bind}\,\,w\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,x;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,w;N)

(
\underline{F}\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,w\leftarrow(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,x);\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,w;N)

(
M
thunkable)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,w\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,w;N)

(
\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M;N)

(
U\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow M;N)

∎

Lemma 5.8 (Return is Thunkable).

If
\Gamma\vdash V:A
then
\texttt{ret}\,\,V
is thunkable.

Proof.

By
\underline{F}\beta
:

\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,V;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,x\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,V

∎

Lemma 5.9 (Complex Values Simplify to Thunkable Terms).

If
\Gamma\vdash V:A
is a (possibly) complex value, then
\Gamma\vdash{V}^{{\dagger}}:\underline{F}A
is thunkable.

Proof.

Introduction forms follow from return is thunkable and thunkables
compose. For elimination forms it is sufficient to show that when
the branches of pattern matching are thunkable, the pattern match
is thunkable.

(1)

x
: We need to show
{x}^{{\dagger}}=\texttt{ret}\,\,x
is thunkable, which we
proved as a lemma above.

(2)

0
elim, we need to show

\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\texttt{absurd}\,\,V;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,y\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,{\texttt{absurd}\,\,V}

but by
\eta 0
both sides are equivalent to
\texttt{absurd}\,\,V
.

(3)

+
elim, we need to show

\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{case}\,\,V\{x_{1}.M_{1}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.M_{2}\})\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow(\texttt{case}\,\,V\{x_{1}.M_{1}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.M_{2}\});\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,y

\displaystyle\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{case}\,\,V\{x_{1}.M_{1}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.M_{2}\})

(
+\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{case}\,\,V

\displaystyle\qquad\{{x_{1}.\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{case}\,\,\texttt{inl}\,\,x_{1}\{x_{1}.M_{1}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.M_{2}\})}

\displaystyle\qquad\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{case}\,\,\texttt{inr}\,\,x_{2}\{x_{1}.M_{1}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.M_{2}\})\}

(
+\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{case}\,\,V

\displaystyle\qquad\{{x_{1}.\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M_{2}\}

(
M_{1},M_{2}
thunkable)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{case}\,\,V

\displaystyle\qquad\{{x_{1}.\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow M_{1};\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,y}

\displaystyle\qquad\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow M_{2};\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,y\}

(commuting conversion)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow(\texttt{case}\,\,V\{x_{1}.M_{1}\,\,|\,\,x_{2}.M_{2}\});\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,y

elim

(
\times\eta

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,z\leftarrow(\texttt{split}\,\,V\,\texttt{to}\,\,(x,y).M);\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,z

∎

Dually, we have that a stack out of a force is linear and that linears
are closed under composition, so we can easily build up bigger linear
morphisms from smaller ones.

Lemma 5.10 (Force to a stack is Linear).

If
\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\bullet:\underline{B}\vdash S:\underline{C}
, then

\Gamma,x:U\underline{B}\vdash S[\texttt{force}\,\,x]:\underline{B}
is linear in
x
.

Proof.

(
U\beta
)

\displaystyle S[\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{force}\,\,x)}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}S[{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{force}\,\,x)}]

(
\underline{F}\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;S[\texttt{force}\,\,x]

∎

Lemma 5.11 (Linear Terms Compose).

If
\Gamma,x:U\underline{B}\vdash M:\underline{B}^{\prime}
is linear in
x
and

\Gamma,y:\underline{B}^{\prime}\vdash N:\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}
is linear in
y
, then

\Gamma,x:U\underline{B}\vdash N[\texttt{thunk}\,\,M/y]:

Proof.

\displaystyle N[\texttt{thunk}\,\,M/y][\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{force}\,\,x)}/x]

\displaystyle=N[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(M[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{force}\,\,x)}])}/y]

(
M
linear)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}N[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;M)}/y]

(
U\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}N[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M)}/y]

(
\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}N[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M;\texttt{force}\,\,y)}/y]

(
\underline{F}\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}N[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M);\texttt{force}\,\,y)}/y]

(
U\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}N[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,w;\texttt{force}\,\,y)}/y][\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M)/w]

(
N
linear)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}(\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,w;N)[\texttt{thunk}\,\,(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M)/w]

(
U\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}(\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M);N)

(
\underline{F}\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,M;N

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;N[\texttt{thunk}\,\,M/y]

∎

Lemma 5.12 (Complex Stacks Simplify to Linear Terms).

If
\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\bullet:\underline{B}\vdash S:\underline{C}
is a (possibly)
complex stack, then
\Gamma,z:U\underline{B}\vdash{({S})}^{{\dagger}}:\underline{C}
is linear in
z
.

Proof.

There are
4
classes of rules for complex stacks: those that are
rules for simple stacks (
\bullet
, computation type elimination
forms), introduction rules for negative computation types where the
subterms are complex stacks, elimination of positive value types
where the continuations are complex stacks and finally application
to a complex value.

The rules for simple stacks are easy: they follow immediately from
the fact that forcing to a stack is linear and that complex stacks
compose. For the negative introduction forms, we have to show that
binding commutes with introduction forms. For pattern matching
forms, we just need commuting conversions. For function application,
we use the lemma that binding a thunkable in a linear term is
linear.

(1)

\bullet
: This is just saying that
\texttt{force}\,\,z
is linear,
which we showed above.

(2)

\to
elim We need to show, assuming that
\Gamma,x:\underline{B}\vdash M:\underline{C}
is linear in
x
and
\Gamma\vdash N:\underline{F}A

is thunkable, that

\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;M\,y

is linear in
x
.

\displaystyle\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;(M[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{force}\,\,x)}/x])\,y

(
M
linear in
x
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;M)\,y

(
\underline{F}\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;M\,y

(thunkables are central)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{bind}\,\,y\leftarrow N;M\,y

(3)

\to
intro

\displaystyle\lambda y:A.M[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{force}\,\,x)}/x]

(
M
is linear)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\lambda y:A.\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;M

(
\to\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\lambda y:A.\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;(\lambda y:A.M)\,y

(
\underline{F}\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\lambda y:A.(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;(\lambda y:A.M))\,y

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;(\lambda y:A.M)

We need to show

\texttt{bind}\,\,w\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\{\}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\{\}

Which is immediate by

\displaystyle\{\pi\mapsto{M[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{force}\,\,x)}]/x}

)

)

\displaystyle\qquad\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{\pi^{\prime}({\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\{\pi\mapsto{M}\,\,|\,\,\pi^{\prime}\mapsto{N}\}})}\}

(
\mathbin{\&}\eta
)

(6)

\nu

\displaystyle\texttt{roll}\,\,M[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;\texttt{force}\,\,x)}/x]

(

is linear)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{roll}\,\,(\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow\texttt{force}\,\,z;M)

(

∎

Composing this with the previous translation from GTT to CBPV*
shows that
GTT value type upcasts are thunkable and computation
type downcasts are linear
.

Since the translation takes values and stacks to terms, it cannot
preserve substitution up to equality.
Rather, we get the following, weaker notion that says that the
translation of a syntactic substitution is equivalent to an effectful
composition.

Lemma 5.13 (Compositionality of De-complexification).

(1)

If
\Gamma,x:A\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash E:T
and
\Gamma\vdash V:A

are complex terms, then

{({E[V/x]})}^{{\dagger}}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{V}^{{\dagger}};{{E}^{{\dagger}}}

(2)

If
\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\bullet:\underline{B}\vdash S:\underline{C}
and
\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash M:\underline{B}
, then

{({S[M]})}^{{\dagger}}\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}{S}^{{\dagger}}[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{M}^{{\dagger}}/z]

Proof.

(1)

First, note that every occurrence of a variable in
{E}^{{\dagger}}
is of
the form
\texttt{ret}\,\,x
for some variable
x
. This means we can define
substitution of a
term
for a variable in a simplified term by
defining
{E}^{{\dagger}}[N/\texttt{ret}\,\,x]
to replace every
\texttt{ret}\,\,x:\underline{F}A

with
N:\underline{F}A
. Then it is an easy observation that
simplification is compositional on the nose with respect to this
notion of substitution:

{({E[V/x]})}^{{\dagger}}={E}^{{\dagger}}[{V}^{{\dagger}}/\texttt{ret}\,\,x]

Next by repeated invocation of
U\beta
,

{E}^{{\dagger}}[{V}^{{\dagger}}/\texttt{ret}\,\,x]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}{E}^{{\dagger}}[\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,{V}^{{\dagger}}/\texttt{ret}\,\,x]

Then we can lift the definition of the thunk to the top-level by
\underline{F}\beta
:

{E}^{{\dagger}}[\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,{V}^{{\dagger}}/\texttt{ret}\,\,x]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,;{V}^{{\dagger}}w{E}^{{\dagger}}[\texttt{force}\,\,w/\texttt{ret}\,\,x]

Then because
{V}^{{\dagger}}
is thunkable, we can bind it at the top-level
and reduce an administrative redex away to get our desired result:

\displaystyle\texttt{bind}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,;{V}^{{\dagger}}w{E}^{{\dagger}}[\texttt{force}\,\,w/\texttt{ret}\,\,x]

(
V
thunkable)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{V}^{{\dagger}};\texttt{bind}\,\,w\leftarrow\texttt{ret}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,x;{E}^{{\dagger}}[\texttt{force}\,\,w/\texttt{ret}\,\,x]

(
\underline{F}\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{V}^{{\dagger}};{E}^{{\dagger}}[\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,\texttt{ret}\,\,x/\texttt{ret}\,\,x]

(
U\beta
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{V}^{{\dagger}};{E}^{{\dagger}}[\texttt{ret}\,\,x/\texttt{ret}\,\,x]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\texttt{bind}\,\,x\leftarrow{V}^{{\dagger}};{E}^{{\dagger}}

(2)

Note that every occurrence of
z
in
{S}^{{\dagger}}
is of the form

\texttt{force}\,\,z
. This means we can define substitution of a
term

M:\underline{B}
for
\texttt{force}\,\,z
in
{S}^{{\dagger}}
by replacing
\texttt{force}\,\,z

with
M
. It is an easy observation that simplification is
compositional on the nose with respect to this notion of
substitution:

{({S[M/\bullet]})}^{{\dagger}}={S}^{{\dagger}}[{M}^{{\dagger}}/\texttt{force}\,\,z]

Then by repeated
U\beta
, we can replace
{M}^{{\dagger}}
with a forced thunk:

{S}^{{\dagger}}[{M}^{{\dagger}}/\texttt{force}\,\,z]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}{S}^{{\dagger}}[\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,{M}^{{\dagger}}/\texttt{force}\,\,z]

which since we are now substituting a force for a force is the
same as substituting the thunk for the variable:

{S}^{{\dagger}}[\texttt{force}\,\,\texttt{thunk}\,\,{M}^{{\dagger}}/\texttt{force}\,\,z]\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}{S}^{{\dagger}}[\texttt{thunk}\,\,{M}^{{\dagger}}/z]

∎

Theorem 5.14 (De-complexification preserves Dynamism).

If
\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash E\sqsubseteq E^{\prime}:T
then
{\Gamma,{\Delta}^{{\dagger}}\vdash{E}^{{\dagger}}\sqsubseteq{E^{\prime}}^{{\dagger}}:{T}^{{\dagger}}}

Proof.

(1)

Reflexivity is translated to reflexivity.

(2)

Transitivity is translated to transitivity.

(3)

Compatibility rules are translated to compatibility rules.

(4)

Substitution of a Value

\frac{\Gamma,x:A,{\Delta}^{{\dagger}}\vdash{E}^{{\dagger}}\sqsubseteq{E^{\prime}}^{{\dagger}}:{T}^{{\dagger}}\Gamma\vdash{V}^{{\dagger}}\sqsubseteq{V^{\prime}}^{{\dagger}}:\underline{F}A}{\Gamma,{\Delta}^{{\dagger}}\vdash{E[V/x]}^{{\dagger}}\sqsubseteq{E^{\prime}[V^{\prime}/x]}^{{\dagger}}:{T}^{{\dagger}}}

which follows by bind compatibility.
